For a word h of length3 let v=T(h)^(-1)e. Its entries\nsatisfy v0=1 and v_(i+1)=(1-h_i)v_i. Since L(Lambda)e=0,\n\n    v^T M(h,Lambda)v=v^T D(h)v=1-A(h)^2*v3^2.               (14)\n\nThe second equality follows from\nh_i(2-h_i)v_i^2=v_i^2-v_(i+1)^2 and -s(s+2)=1-A^2.\nAt the base, M=0, A=lambda^2 and v3=-u^2. Differentiating(14) therefore\ngives the necessary equality\n\n    (r0+r1+r2)/lambda^2+lambda*(r0+r2)+r1=0.               (15)\n\nThe moving-vector derivative contributes0 because the base matrix is0.\nSubstituting r0=0 and r2=2lambda*r1 into(15) gives\n\n    [(1+2lambda)/lambda^2+2lambda^2+1]r1=0.\n\nThe bracket is strictly positive, so r1=r2=0. Equations(13) give\ny13=y31=y21=0. Entry22 now reads y21-u*y23=0, so y23=0; flow at2 gives\ny21+y23-y32=0, so y32=0. Together with(12) and y03=0, this proves that\nthe full derivative is zero. No sign assumption on these eight derivatives\nwas needed. This additional03 entry is retained because it arises when the\nright child of the seven-step array is extracted.\n\n## 4. Exact central-cut algebra to first order\n\nAssume p,Y satisfy f=0, W=0, and Y=0 on Z0. Thus all coefficient derivatives\nare supported on E58. Introduce the REAL, not assumed feasible, families\n\n    h(t)=hbar+t*p, Lambda(t)=Lambdabar+t*Y.\n\nTheir flow holds exactly for every real t. Their full remainder is O(t^2),\nentry by entry, because its constant and linear coefficients are0. Here\nO(t^2) means a function with absolute value bounded by a fixed multiple of\nt^2 for sufficiently small |t|; the constants may depend on the fixed p,Y.\nAll computations in this section retain constant and first-order terms.\n\nLet P58={0,1,2,3}, Bset={4,5,6,7}, and let eP,eB be their indicator columns\nin R8. Define capacities and bridge excess from these ACTUAL physical steps:\n\n    a(t)=1+h0+h1+h2, b(t)=1+h4+h5+h6, d(t)=h3-1.\n\nWrite h_left=(h0,h1,h2) and h_right=(h4,h5,h6). At t=0, a=b=lambda^2 and\nd=lambda. The only P58-to-Bset coefficient is\nN(t)=Lambda37. The only Bset-to-P58 coefficients are mu_j(t)=Lambda_(j,3)\nfor j=4,5,6,7. This support follows from(10) and the displayed E58, rather\nthan from an ansatz about general feasible certificates.\n\nLet LambdaL be the P58-by-P58 subarray, interpreted as a three-step certificate\narray for h0,h1,h2. Its original flow holds exactly: the first three rows\nhave no crossing edge, and the terminal flow follows by summing all four\nrow-minus-column sums. Direct matrix multiplication gives\n\n    M(h,Lambda)_(P58,P58)\n      =M(h_left,LambdaL)+(a^2-d^2-d*N)diag(0,0,0,1).        (16)\n\nIndeed the only crossing contribution within this principal block is\nN*(T_3-T_7) in row3; it changes only entry33, by -d*N. The parent-versus-child\nD difference at that entry is (1-d^2)-(1-a^2)=a^2-d^2. The other entries\nagree. Thus every derivative entry of M(h_left,LambdaL) except possibly33\nis0. The scalar flow identity e^T M e=0 from Section2 also holds for this\nchild at every t, forcing the remaining derivative entry to vanish. Hence\n\n    M(h_left,LambdaL)=O(t^2), a^2-d^2-d*N=O(t^2).            (17)\n\nFor j in Bset, direct multiplication by eP yields\n\n    2(M eP)_j=d*mu_j-N*h_j, j=4,5,6,\n    2(M eP)_7=d*mu_7-N.                                  (18)\n\nTo see every contribution in(18), T eP equals a+d on Bset and a at index3,\nso (L T eP)_j=d*mu_j. On Bset, L^T eP has only the entry -N at7.\nUpper triangular multiplication by T^T therefore contributes -N*h_j\nfor j<7 and -N at7. D eP vanishes on Bset. This proves(18) without\nany positivity or remainder-rank hypothesis.\n\nSince M=O(t^2) and d stays positive, (18) gives\n\n    mu_j=(N/d)h_j+O(t^2), j=4,5,6;\n    mu_7=N/d+O(t^2); sum_j mu_j=N*b/d+O(t^2).\n\nSumming the exact original flow over P58 gives\n\n    sum_j mu_j-N=2(a+d).\n\nIt follows that\n\n    N(b-d)=2d(a+d)+O(t^2).                                (19)\n\nMultiplying(19) by d and using d*N=a^2-d^2+O(t^2) from(17), then dividing\nby the positive a+d, gives\n\n    (a-d)(b-d)=2d^2+O(t^2),\n    a*b-d(a+b)-d^2=O(t^2).                                (20)\n\nThis saturated-bridge relation has been DERIVED for the zero-remainder\nderivative; it was not imposed on the full tangent cone.\n\n## 5. Extraction of both complete children and the full equality kernel\n\nFor positive a,b near lambda^2 define the positive root\n\n    dhat=(-(a+b)+sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab))/2,\n    Cjoin=a+b+dhat, kappa=(Cjoin/b)^2.\n\nThese functions are twice continuously differentiable for a,b in a sufficiently\nsmall positive neighborhood of their base values, so all derivatives below exist.\n\nThe identity a*b=dhat*Cjoin is exact. Equation(20) and the factorization\n\n    a*b-d(a+b)-d^2=-(d-dhat)(a+b+d+dhat)\n\nshow d-dhat=O(t^2). Consequently (17)--(19) yield\n\n    N=dhat*(kappa-1)+O(t^2),\n    mu_j=(kappa-1)h_j+O(t^2), j=4,5,6,\n    mu_7=kappa-1+O(t^2).                                  (21)\n\nFor the first equality use N=(a^2-d^2)/d+O(t^2), replace d by dhat, and\nuse a/dhat=Cjoin/b. All divisions have nonzero positive base values.\n\nDefine a real child array on Bset, with local indices0,...,3, by\n\n    LambdaR_ij=Lambda_(i,j)/kappa\n       -(1-1/kappa)h_i*1_(i<7,j=7), i,j in Bset, i!=j.     (22)\n\nKeep its diagonal0. No sign assumption on LambdaR is made. Its nonterminal\nflow is -2h_j+O(t^2): the RR subarray's flow at j<7 equals -2h_j-mu_j;\nafter division and subtraction in(22) it is\n\n    (-2h_j-mu_j)/kappa-(1-1/kappa)h_j=-2h_j+O(t^2).\n\nThe terminal flow then follows by summing the four row-minus-column sums.\n\nHere is the exact full-space composition identity used to extract remainders.\nFor our two physical child words of capacities a,b and bridge1+dhat, the\nbridge index is3 and the terminal index is7. Let j range over4,5,6, the right\nchild's nonterminal indices. Accepted fact04219554e41d9750, Section11, gives\n\n    V_parent=V_left+kappa*V_right-(kappa-1)*Ejoin,\n    Ejoin=sum_j h_j Q_(j,3)+Q_(7,3)+dhat Q_(3,7)\n                                             +sum_j h_j Q_(j,7). (23)\n\nIts coefficient proof uses dhat*(a+b+dhat)=ab and kappa=((a+b+dhat)/b)^2.\nThus the joined multiplier array consists of LambdaL on P58, kappa*LambdaR\non Bset plus (kappa-1)h_i into7 from each nonterminal right index, and\nthe cross multipliers dhat(kappa-1), (kappa-1)h_j, kappa-1. Under child flow\nthe FULL remainder is the disjoint original-coordinate block sum\nM_left plus kappa*M_right. This is an algebraic coefficient identity; its\nvalidity does not require the child arrays to be nonnegative or their\nremainders to be PSD. In the current application child flow holds through\nfirst order, which is precisely the order at which we use this identity.\nMore explicitly, for an arbitrary real child array with flow defect\nxi=L(Lambda)^T e-omega, collecting the same coefficients as in(2) gives\n\n    V+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij=trace(M Gram)\n                           +sum_i xi_i(f_i-||g_i||^2/2).\n\nThe function-value coefficients gain xi_i and the gradient diagonal gains\n-xi_i/2. This exact formula shows explicitly that an O(t^2) flow defect\ncannot alter the constant or linear matrix coefficients in the join identity.\n\nReplace the bridge by1+dhat and crossings by the exact expressions in(23).\nBy(21) these changes are O(t^2); the internal arrays are unchanged. Combining\nthe original identity(2) with(23), or just collecting its gradient coefficients,\ntherefore gives\n\n    M(h,Lambda)=M(h_left,LambdaL) direct-sum\n                   kappa*M(h_right,LambdaR)+O(t^2).        (24)\n\nThe flow errors in(22) are O(t^2), so cannot alter a constant or linear\ncoefficient in this equality. Equations(17) and(24) imply the right child\nremainder is also O(t^2). Thus both children have exact base flow, zero\nbase remainder, correct derivative flow, and zero derivative remainder.\n\nThe left child's derivative support is the seven pairs in(11). The right\nchild's support is those same seven pairs plus03. Explicitly its original\ninternal allowed pairs are45,47,54,57,65,67,75,76; the subtractions in(22)\nonly affect47,57,67. Hence no other pair is introduced. At the base,\nkappa=lambda^2 and1-1/kappa=2/lambda. Substitution in(22) gives\n\n    LambdaR_01=2, LambdaR_03=0,\n    LambdaR_10=LambdaR_13=LambdaR_31=2lambda,\n    LambdaR_21=2lambda*q58,\n    LambdaR_23=2lambda^2+2lambda*q58,\n    LambdaR_32=2lambda^3.\n\nFor example its03 entry is (2lambda*q58)/lambda^2-(2/lambda)q58=0;\nits13 entry is (2lambda^3+4lambda)/lambda^2-(2/lambda)*2=2lambda;\nits23 entry is\n(2lambda^4+2lambda^3*q58+2lambda*q58)/lambda^2-(2/lambda)q58\n=2lambda^2+2lambda*q58.\nThe other five listed entries are obtained by division by lambda^2.\n\nApply Section3 separately to these two complete four-gradient derivatives.\nIt gives\n\n    p0=p1=p2=p4=p5=p6=0,\n    LambdaL'(0)=LambdaR'(0)=0.\n\nDifferentiating(20) now leaves\n\n    -(2lambda^2+2lambda)*p3=0,\n\nso p3=0. Then a'=b'=dhat'=kappa'=0. Equations(21) give N'=mu_j'=0,\nand differentiating(22) gives all original RR coefficient derivatives0.\nThe PP and crossing derivatives are already0. Consequently\n\n    f=0, W=0, Y|Z0=0  ==>  p=0 and Y=0.                   (25)\n\nThis conclusion was obtained by textual exact algebra only, with no rank\ncomputation, machine elimination or extra mathematical job. Together with(10)\nit identifies the FULL cone's zero-capacity equality set:\n\n    (p,Y) in C58 and S=0  iff  (p,Y)=(0,0).                (26)\n\n## 6. Uniqueness at the base, rederived from the same joint identity\n\nLet Lambda be ANY full feasible array at hbar. Put p=0 and\nY=Lambda-Lambdabar. The full flow gives f=0. Because p=0, no nonlinear term\noccurs: W(0,Y)=M(hbar,Lambda). On each of the36 zero base entries,\nY_ij=Lambda_ij>=0. Thus (0,Y) belongs to C58 and has S=0. Equation(26)\ngives Y=0. We have proved\n\n    Lambda feasible at hbar ==> Lambda=Lambdabar.          (27)\n\nThis proof covers every base certificate and every PSD rank. It does not\nassume the all-base uniqueness claim of the earlier b937bb05ba1645b1 draft.\n\n## 7. Uniform bounds and convergence for arbitrary nearby certificate choices\n\nWork inside the compact cube h_i in[1,4], which contains hbar in its interior.\nConsider the ONE scalar function F(x)=k*x^2/2, with k=1/8, minimizer0 and\nx0=1. Its actual GD positions satisfy\n\n    x_i=product_(j<i)(1-h_j/8), 1/2<=1-h_j/8<=7/8.\n\nThey strictly decrease and stay positive. If0<=i<j<=7, then\n\n    x_i-x_j >= x_i-x_(i+1)=(h_i/8)x_i >=2^-9,\n\nbecause i<=6 and x_i>=2^-6. Direct expansion of all ordered residuals gives\n\n    Q_ij=-k(1-k)(x_i-x_j)^2/2 <=-7/2^25 for i!=j.           (28)\n\nThis checks every original ordered finite pair for the same actual quadratic.\nIts gradients are g_i=k*x_i. In V_h, all terms h_i(1-h_i)g_i^2 and the\nterminal negative square are nonpositive, whereas f_i-f7<=f_i<=k/2.\nSince sum_i h_i<=28, we have V_h<=28k=7/2. Applying(2) to any feasible\nLambda, using full PSD and all its nonnegative entries, gives\n\n    (7/2^25)sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij\n       <=sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij(-Q_ij)<=V_h<=7/2.\n\nHence EVERY certificate in this cube satisfies\n\n    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij<=2^24.                             (29)\n\nThe bound is intentionally coarse and purely analytic. In particular the\nentire set of coefficient arrays is bounded independently of their selection.\n\nNow take any sequence h_k->hbar and choose any feasible Lambda_k. Eventually\nthe words lie in the cube. A subsequence of the arrays converges by(29).\nThe limit is nonnegative with zero diagonal, has the exact base flow, and has\na PSD base remainder by continuity: each quadratic form is a limit of\nnonnegative quadratic forms. Equation(27) identifies the limit as Lambdabar.\nIf uniform convergence over all selections failed, one could choose such a\nsequence with ||Lambda_k-Lambdabar||_F bounded away from0; its convergent\nsubsequence would contradict(27). Therefore\n\n    h->hbar through feasible words implies Lambda->Lambdabar\n    uniformly over ALL feasible choices of Lambda.         (30)\n\nNo estimate on ||Lambda-Lambdabar||/||h-hbar|| was used. Such quotients are\nallowed to be unbounded at this stage of the argument.\n\n## 8. Exact nonlinear remainder and strict local capacity loss\n\nFor arbitrary finite displacements p=h-hbar, Y=Lambda-Lambdabar, the full\npolynomial expansion is EXACT:\n\n    M(h,Lambda)=W(p,Y)+R2(p,Y),\n    R2(p,Y)=diag(-p0^2,...,-p6^2,-S^2)+sym(L(Y)Tp).          (31)\n\nFlow is exactly f(p,Y)=0. Every original sign is retained:\nY_ij>=0 on Z0 and Lambdabar_ij+Y_ij>=0 on E58. There are no omitted terms in\n(31). In particular the mixed coefficient-step term is retained, and higher\norders arising from a nonlinear choice of h,Lambda are already covered by\nthis exact displacement formula.\n\nPut ||(p,Y)||_* = sqrt(||p||_2^2+||Y||_F^2). Cauchy--Schwarz gives\n\n    ||L(Y)||_F <=(sqrt(7)+1)||Y||_F,\n    ||Tp||_F <=sqrt(7)||p||_2,\n    ||diag(-p_i^2,-S^2)||_F <=||p||_2^2+S^2<=8||p||_2^2.\n\nThe first bound uses seven off-diagonal entries in each row. The second uses\n||Tp||_F^2=sum_(j=0)^6(7-j)p_j^2. Symmetrization does not increase Frobenius\nnorm, and the Frobenius norm of a product is bounded by the product of norms.\nConsequently\n\n    ||R2(p,Y)||_F <=8||p||_2^2+(7+sqrt(7))||p||_2||Y||_F\n                   <=13||(p,Y)||_*^2.                     (32)\n\nDefine the compact set\n\n    K58={(p,Y) in C58: ||(p,Y)||_*=1}.\n\nIt is closed because flow and W are linear and the sign and PSD cones are\nclosed; it lies on a finite-dimensional unit sphere. It is nonempty, as the\nfollowing actual feasible path confirms without a new computation. For\n0<=t<q58 let x=q58-t. The singleton word x with multipliers2 and2(1+x) has the\nfull2-by2 remainder(2-x^2)[[1,-1],[-1,1]], which is PSD. This follows by\nsubstituting L=[[2,-2],[-2(1+x),2(1+x)]] and T=[[1,0],[x,1]] into D+sym(LT).\nJoin two copies at bridge\n1+u(1+x), then two resulting children at bridge2+x. The accepted join has\npositive coefficients, fixed right scaling lambda^2 at both levels, and\nactual word\n\n    (x,1+u(1+x),x,2+x,x,1+u(1+x),x).\n\nIts base array is Lambdabar; all coefficients are differentiable in t.\nIts tangent has p=(-1,-u,-1,-1,-1,-u,-1) and S=-lambda^2, so is nonzero\nand belongs to C58. Normalize it to obtain a point of K58.\n\nEquations(7),(26) show -S>0 everywhere on K58. Hence\n\n    m58=min_((p,Y) in K58)(-sum_i p_i)>0, c=m58/2>0.        (33)\n\nThe minimum is a mathematical definition, not a solver output or a new\ncomputation. Its positivity follows from compactness: a sequence of unit\ncone points with -S->0 would have a unit limit in the equality set(26).\n\nWe now prove that all sufficiently small FEASIBLE joint displacements obey\n\n    -S >=c||(p,Y)||_*.                                    (34)\n\nIf this failed, choose nonzero feasible displacements (p_k,Y_k) with\nt_k=||(p_k,Y_k)||_*->0 and -S_k<c*t_k. Normalize\n(p_k,Y_k)=t_k*(p_hat_k,Y_hat_k). A subsequence of the unit normalized pairs\nconverges to a pair(p_hat,Y_hat) of norm1. Exact flow and all36 active signs\npass to the limit. Homogeneity of(31) gives\n\n    M(hbar+p_k,Lambdabar+Y_k)/t_k\n       =W(p_hat_k,Y_hat_k)+t_k*R2(p_hat_k,Y_hat_k)>=0.\n\nBy(32), the second term tends to0 in norm. Thus W(p_hat,Y_hat)>=0 on the\nFULL R8, so the unit limit lies in K58. The assumed failure of(34) gives\n-sum_i p_hat_i<=c=m58/2, contradicting(33). This proves the existence of\na joint radius r58>0 on which(34) holds for every feasible pair.\n\nFinally use the uniform convergence(30) and h->hbar to choose epsilon>0\nsuch that EVERY feasible array above a word with ||h-hbar||_2<epsilon\nsatisfies ||(h-hbar,Lambda-Lambdabar)||_*<r58. The cube condition can be\nensured by making epsilon smaller, since hbar is in its interior. Equation\n(34) is then exactly the asserted capacity bound(3). At the base it holds\nwith both sides0. Cauchy--Schwarz also gives\n\n    c||Lambda-Lambdabar||_F <=Abar-A(h)\n                          =-sum_i(h_i-hbar_i)\n                          <=sqrt(7)||h-hbar||_2,\n\nwhich proves(4).\n\nThis normalization uses the JOINT displacement, so it never assumes bounded\nphysical coefficient difference quotients. Singular PSD faces and zero leading\nsigns cause no gap: PSD is passed to a limit after the full exact quadratic\nerror vanishes, and equality of capacity at a unit limiting tangent is\nexcluded by the explicit kernel calculation. No Hessian coercivity, smooth\ncertificate selection, or sufficiency of a truncated second-order path is\nassumed.\n\n## 9. self.a * other.b + self.b * other.a)\n\n    __rmul__ = __mul__\n\n    def __truediv__(self, other):\n        other = Q.of(other)\n        den = other.a * other.a - 2 * other.b * other.b\n        if den == 0:\n            raise ZeroDivisionError('exact zero field divisor')\n        return Q((self.a * other.a - 2 * self.b * other.b) / den,\n                 (self.b * other.a - self.a * other.b) / den)\n\n    def __pow__(self, exponent):\n        result = Q(1)\n        for _ in range(exponent):\n            result = result * self\n        return result\n\n    def __bool__(self):\n        return bool(self.a or self.b)\n\n    def __eq__(self, other):\n        other = Q.of(other)\n        return self.a == other.a and self.b == other.b\n\n    def encoded(self):\n        return [str(self.a), str(self.b)]\n\n    def sign(self):\n        a, b = self.a, self.b\n        if a == 0:\n            return (b > 0) - (b < 0)\n        if b == 0:\n            return (a > 0) - (a < 0)\n        if (a > 0) == (b > 0):\n            return 1 if a > 0 else -1\n        difference = a * a - 2 * b * b\n        return ((a > 0) - (a < 0)) * ((difference > 0) - (difference < 0))\n\n\ndef encode(x):\n    if isinstance(x, Q):\n        return x.encoded()\n    if isinstance(x, list):\n        return [encode(v) for v in x]\n    if isinstance(x, dict):\n        return {k: encode(v) for k, v in x.items()}\n    return x\n\n\ndef matrix(rows, cols):\n    return [[Q() for _ in range(cols)] for _ in range(rows)]\n\n\ndef multiply(A, B):\n    C = matrix(len(A), len(B[0]))\n    for i, row in enumerate(A):\n        for k, value in enumerate(row):\n            if value:\n                for j, other in enumerate(B[k]):\n                    if other:\n                        C[i][j] = C[i][j] + value * other\n    return C\n\n\ndef laplacian(X):\n    A = [[-x for x in row] for row in X]\n    for i in range(len(X)):\n        A[i][i] = sum(X[i], Q())\n    return A\n\n\ndef check(name, condition, detail=None):\n main():\n    data = json.loads((ROOT / 'frozen_exact_data.json').read_text())\n    # Literal definitions from the mathematical manuscript, not from its checker.\n    q = Q(0, 1)\n    ell = 1 + q\n    h = [q, Q(2), q, 1 + ell, q, Q(2), q]\n    capacity = ell ** 3\n    table = {\n        (0, 1): Q(2), (1, 0): 2 * ell, (1, 3): 2 * ell,\n        (2, 1): 2 * ell * q, (2, 3): 2 * ell ** 2 + 2 * ell * 7): 2 * ell ** 3 + 4 * ell,\n        (6, 3): 2 * ell * q, (6, 5): 2 * ell ** 3 * q,\n        (6, 7): 2 * ell ** 4 + 2 * ell ** 3 * q + 2 * ell * q,\n        (7, 3): 2 * ell, (7, 5): 2 * ell ** 3, (7, 6): 2 * ell ** 5,\n    }\n    pairs = [(i, j) for i in range(8) for j in range(8) if i != j]\n    zeros = [ij for ij in pairs if ij not in table]\n    base = matrix(8, 8)\n    for (i, j), value in table.items():\n        base[i][j] = value\n    L0 = laplacian(base)\n    T0 = [[Q(1) if i == j else (h[j] if j < i else Q()) for j in range(8)] for i in range(8)]\n    s = sum(h, Q())\n    omega = [-2 * x for x in h] + [2 * s]\n    base_flow = [sum((L0[i][j] for i in range(8)), Q()) - omega[j] for j in range(8)]\n    LT = multiply(L0, T0)\n    D0 = [x * (2 - x) for x in h] + [-s * (s + 2)]\n    M0 = [[(LT[i][j] + LT[j][i]) / 2 + (D0[i] if i == j else Q()) for j in range(8)] for i in range(8)]\n    check('seven steps and exact base capacity', capacity == 1 + s, h)\n    check('all twenty base entries positive', len(table) == 20 and all(v.sign() == 1 for v in table.values()))\n    check('base original flow', all(not x for x in base_flow), base_flow)\n    check('base full remainder is zero', all(not x for row in M0 for x in row), M0)\n\n    equalities = [Q.of(v) for v in data['equality_prices']]\n    check('sixteen equality prices', len(equalities) == 16)\n    a, b = equalities[:8], For every input it checked(7),\ntrace(ZB)=trace(HW), the q58-weighted svec inner product, and\n\n    e^T W e=f^T Tbal58 e.\n\nIts complete result retains all original f,W,We,B arrays. Every coefficient\nresidual is the rational pair(0,0). Source, input and expected-check hashes\nwere frozen before the sole charged job. No supplied passed flag or supplied\nstationarity residual was used; no author checker was imported.\n\nField arithmetic uses pairs(r,s) representing r+s*q58, with\n(r,s)*(a,b)=(ra+2sb,rb+sa). Inversion multiplies by the conjugate and divides\nby r^2-2s^2. Every denominator check is exact. For signs, if r,s have the\nsame nonzero sign use that sign; if they have opposite signs use\nsign(r)*sign(r^2-2s^2). Zero-coordinate cases are immediate. Appendix A's\n36 sigma values all have sign+1 by this exact rule.\n\nAppendix A also gives Z=R diag(d0,...,d6) R^T. The independent readback checks\nthat R is unit lower triangular, every one of the49 product entries is Z's\ncorresponding entry, and all seven d_i have exact sign+1. Thus Z is positive\ndefinite. No floating eigenvalue is a premise. Let r_k be column k of R, a\nvector of length7. Since\n\n    trace(ZB)=sum_k d_k r_k^T B r_k,\n\nthis trace is nonnegative for PSD B. If it vanishes, each of the seven\nnonnegative quadratic forms vanishes; the PSD argument used for the common\nnull then makes B annihilate the basis formed by the columns of R. Hence B=0.\n\nFor(6), f=n=0, B>=0, and every active Y is nonnegative. Identity(7) proves\nS<=0. If S=0, strict positivity of every sigma and positive definiteness of Z\ngive\n\n    Y_ij=0 for every ij in Z0, B=0, W=0.                    (10)\n\nUp to this point a nonzero equality direction has NOT been excluded. The\nnext two sections establish its exclusion by original-coordinate algebra.\n\n## 3. A three-step zero-remainder derivative calculation\n\nUse local indices0,1,2,3 and the three-step base word(q58,2,q58), of capacity\nlambda^2. In this section L,T,omega,D,M are defined exactly as in Section1\nwith three steps and four gradients, so the terminal index is3 and e is the\nall-ones column of R4. Its base array has seven positive entries\n\n    01=2, 10=13=31=2lambda, 21=2lambda*q58,\n    23=2lambda^2+2lambda*q58, 32=2lambda^3,                    (11)\n\nand all other entries0. Its remainder is0 by the accepted singleton join.\nPermit derivatives on exactly these seven pairs AND the additional pair03.\nThe latter derivative is an unrestricted real number. Write r0,r1,r2 for\nthe physical derivatives, y_ij for the coefficient derivatives, and assume\nthe four original flow derivatives hold and the full4-by4 remainder\nderivative Wloc is0. We prove all r and y are0.\n\nOnly the pairs in(11) and03 are used in the following displayed entries.\nThe entry02 of L(Lambda)T is -h2*Lambda03, whereas its entry20 is0.\nTherefore\n\n    (Wloc)_02=-q58*y03/2,\n\nwhich forces y03=0. The flow at0 and the entries00 and01 now give\n\n    y01-y10=-2r0,\n    -2q*r0-u*y01=0,\n    -y01+2lambda*r0+u*y10=0.                               (12)\n\nThe first two equations give y10=y01+2r0 and y01=-2q*lambda*r0.\nSubstitution in the last equation yields\n\n    [-2q*lambda*(u-1)+4q]r0=4lambda*r0=0.\n\nThus r0=y01=y10=0. Entries11 and13 and flow at1 give, in order,\n\n    y13=-2(1+lambda)r1,\n    y31=y13-2lambda*r1,\n    y21=2(1+lambda)r1.                                     (13)\n\nFor clarity, these equations come from\n\n    (Wloc)_11=-2(1+lambda)r1-y13,\n    2(Wloc)_13=-y13+y31+2lambda*r1,\n    y13-y21-y31=-2r1.\n\nThe12 entry, obtained from -h2*Lambda13+(h1-1)*Lambda21, gives\n\n    0=-q58*y13-2lambda*r2+y21+2lambda*q58*r1\n      =4lambda^2*r1-2lambda*r2.\n\nConsequently r2=2lambda*r1.\n\nA scalar identity supplies the last physical equation without eliminating\nany formal gradient. For a word h of length3 let v=T(h)^(-1)e. Its entries\nsatisfy v0=1 and v_(i+1)=(1-h_i)v_i. Since L(Lambda)e=0,\n\n    v^T M(h,Lambda)v=v^T D(h)v=1-A(h)^2*v3^2.               (14)\n\nThe second equality follows from\nh_i(2-h_i)v_i^2=v_i^2-v_(i+1)^2 and -s(s+2)=1-A^2.\nAt the base, M=0, A=lambda^2 and v3=-u^2. Differentiating(14) therefore\ngives the necessary equality\n\n    (r0+r1+r2)/lambda^2+lambda*(r0+r2)+r1=0.               (15)\n\nThe moving-vector derivative contributes0 because the base matrix is0.\nSubstituting r0=0 and r2=2lambda*r1 into(15) gives\n\n    [(1+2lambda)/lambda^2+2lambda^2+1]r1=0.\n\nThe bracket is strictly positive, so r1=r2=0. Equations(13) give\ny13=y31=y21=0. Entry22 now reads y21-u*y23=0, so y23=0; flow at2 gives\ny21+y23-y32=0, so y32=0. Together with(12) and y03=0, this proves that\nthe full derivative is zero. No sign assumption on these eight derivatives\nwas needed. This additional03 entry is retained because it arises when the\nright child of the seven-step array is extracted.\n\n## 4. Exact central-cut algebra to first order\n\nAssume p,Y satisfy f=0, W=0, and Y=0 on Z0. Thus all coefficient derivatives\nare supported on E58. Introduce the REAL, not assumed feasible, families\n\n    h(t)=hbar+t*p, Lambda(t)=Lambdabar+t*Y.\n\nTheir flow holds exactly for every real t. Their full remainder is O(t^2),\nentry by entry, because its constant and linear coefficients are0. Here\nO(t^2) means a function with absolute value bounded by a fixed multiple of\nt^2 for sufficiently small |t|; the constants may depend on the fixed p,Y.\nAll computations in this section retain constant and first-order terms.\n\nLet P58={0,1,2,3}, Bset={4,5,6,7}, and let eP,eB be their indicator columns\nin R8. Define capacities and bridge excess from these ACTUAL physical steps:\n\n    a(t)=1+h0+h1+h2, b(t)=1+h4+h5+h6, d(t)=h3-1.\n\nWrite h_left=(h0,h1,h2) and h_right=(h4,h5,h6). At t=0, a=b=lambda^2 and\nd=lambda. The only P58-to-Bset coefficient is\nN(t)=Lambda37. The only Bset-to-P58 coefficients are mu_j(t)=Lambda_(j,3)\nfor j=4,5,6,7. This support follows from(10) and the displayed E58, rather\nthan from an ansatz about general feasible certificates.\n\nLet LambdaL be the P58-by-P58 subarray, interpreted as a three-step certificate\narray for h0,h1,h2. Its original flow holds exactly: the first three rows\nhave no crossing edge, and the terminal flow follows by summing all four\nrow-minus-column sums. Direct matrix multiplication gives\n\n    M(h,Lambda)_(P58,P58)\n      =M(h_left,LambdaL)+(a^2-d^2-d*N)diag(0,0,0,1).        (16)\n\nIndeed the only crossing contribution within this principal block is\nN*(T_3-T_7) in row3; it changes only entry33, by -d*N. The parent-versus-child\nD difference at that entry is (1-d^2)-(1-a^2)=a^2-d^2. The other entries\nagree. Thus every derivative entry of M(h_left,LambdaL) except possibly33\nis0. The scalar flow identity e^T M e=0 from Section2 also holds for this\nchild at every t, forcing the remaining derivative entry to vanish. Hence\n\n    M(h_left,LambdaL)=O(t^2), a^2-d^2-d*N=O(t^2).            (17)\n\nFor j in Bset, direct multiplication by eP yields\n\n    2(M eP)_j=d*mu_j-N*h_j, j=4,5,6,\n    2(M eP)_7=d*mu_7-N.                                  (18)\n\nTo see every contribution in(18), T eP equals a+d on Bset and a at index3,\nso (L T eP)_j=d*mu_j. On Bset, L^T eP has only the entry -N at7.\nUpper triangular multiplication by T^T therefore contributes -N*h_j\nfor j<7 and -N at7. D eP vanishes on Bset. This proves(18) without\nany positivity or remainder-rank hypothesis.\n\nSince M=O(t^2) and d stays positive, (18) gives\n\n    mu_j=(N/d)h_j+O(t^2), j=4,5,6;\n    mu_7=N/d+O(t^2); sum_j mu_j=N*b/d+O(t^2).\n\nSumming the exact original flow over P58 gives\n\n    sum_j mu_j-N=2(a+d).\n\nIt follows that\n\n    N(b-d)=2d(a+d)+O(t^2).                                (19)\n\nMultiplying(19) by d and using d*N=a^2-d^2+O(t^2) from(17), then dividing\nby the positive a+d, gives\n\n    (a-d)(b-d)=2d^2+O(t^2),\n    a*b-d(a+b)-d^2=O(t^2).                                (20)\n\nThis saturated-bridge relation has been DERIVED for the zero-remainder\nderivative; it was not imposed on the full tangent cone.\n\n## 5. Extraction of both complete children and the full equality kernel\n\nFor positive a,b near lambda^2 define the positive root\n\n    dhat=(-(a+b)+sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab))/2,\n    Cjoin=a+b+dhat, kappa=(Cjoin/b)^2.\n\nThese functions are twice continuously differentiable for a,b in a sufficiently\nsmall positive neighborhood of their base values, so all derivatives below exist.\n\nThe identity a*b=dhat*Cjoin is exact. Equation(20) and the factorization\n\n    a*b-d(a+b)-d^2=-(d-dhat)(a+b+d+dhat)\n\nshow d-dhat=O(t^2). Consequently (17)--(19) yield\n\n    N=dhat*(kappa-1)+O(t^2),\n    mu_j=(kappa-1)h_j+O(t^2), j=4,5,6,\n    mu_7=kappa-1+O(t^2).                                  (21)\n\nFor the first equality use N=(a^2-d^2)/d+O(t^2), replace d by dhat, and\nuse a/dhat=Cjoin/b. All divisions have nonzero positive base values.\n\nDefine a real child array on Bset, with local indices0,...,3, by\n\n    LambdaR_ij=Lambda_(i,j)/kappa\n       -(1-1/kappa)h_i*1_(i<7,j=7), i,j in Bset, i!=j.     (22)\n\nKeep its diagonal0. No sign assumption on LambdaR is made. Its nonterminal\nflow is -2h_j+O(t^2): the RR subarray's flow at j<7 equals -2h_j-mu_j;\nafter division and subtraction in(22) it is\n\n    (-2h_j-mu_j)/kappa-(1-1/kappa)h_j=-2h_j+O(t^2).\n\nThe terminal flow then follows by summing the four row-minus-column sums.\n\nHere is the exact full-space composition identity used to extract remainders.\nFor our two physical child words of capacities a,b and bridge1+dhat, the\nbridge index is3 and the terminal index is7. Let j range over4,5,6, the right\nchild's nonterminal indices. Accepted fact04219554e41d9750, Section11, gives\n\n    V_parent=V_left+kappa*V_right-(kappa-1)*Ejoin,\n    Ejoin=sum_j h_j Q_(j,3)+Q_(7,3)+dhat Q_(3,7)\n                                             +sum_j h_j Q_(j,7). (23)\n\nIts coefficient proof uses dhat*(a+b+dhat)=ab and kappa=((a+b+dhat)/b)^2.\nThus the joined multiplier array consists of LambdaL on P58, kappa*LambdaR\non Bset plus (kappa-1)h_i into7 from each nonterminal right index, and\nthe cross multipliers dhat(kappa-1), (kappa-1)h_j, kappa-1. Under child flow\nthe FULL remainder is the disjoint original-coordinate block sum\nM_left plus kappa*M_right. This is an algebraic coefficient identity; its\nvalidity does not require the child arrays to be nonnegative or their\nremainders to be PSD. In the current application child flow holds through\nfirst order, which is precisely the order at which we use this identity.\nMore explicitly, for an arbitrary real child array with flow defect\nxi=L(Lambda)^T e-omega, collecting the same coefficients as in(2) gives\n\n    V+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij=trace(M Gram)\n                           +sum_i xi_i(f_i-||g_i||^2/2).\n\nThe function-value coefficients gain xi_i and the gradient diagonal gains\n-xi_i/2. This exact formula shows explicitly that an O(t^2) flow defect\ncannot alter the constant or linear matrix coefficients in the join identity.\n\nReplace the bridge by1+dhat and crossings by the exact expressions in(23).\nBy(21) these changes are O(t^2); the internal arrays are unchanged. Combining\nthe original identity(2) with(23), or just collecting its gradient coefficients,\ntherefore gives\n\n    M(h,Lambda)=M(h_left,LambdaL) direct-sum\n                   kappa*M(h_right,LambdaR)+O(t^2).        (24)\n\nThe flow errors in(22) are O(t^2), so cannot alter a constant or linear\ncoefficient in this equality. Equations(17) and(24) imply the right child\nremainder is also O(t^2). Thus both children have exact base flow, zero\nbase remainder, correct derivative flow, and zero derivative remainder.\n\nThe left child's derivative support is the seven pairs in(11). The right\nchild's support is those same seven pairs plus03. Explicitly its original\ninternal allowed pairs are45,47,54,57,65,67,75,76; the subtractions in(22)\nonly affect47,57,67. Hence no other pair is introduced. At the base,\nkappa=lambda^2 and1-1/kappa=2/lambda. Substitution in(22) gives\n\n    LambdaR_01=2, LambdaR_03=0,\n    LambdaR_10=LambdaR_13=LambdaR_31=2lambda,\n    LambdaR_21=2lambda*q58,\n    LambdaR_23=2lambda^2+2lambda*q58,\n    LambdaR_32=2lambda^3.\n\nFor example its03 entry is (2lambda*q58)/lambda^2-(2/lambda)q58=0;\nits13 entry is (2lambda^3+4lambda)/lambda^2-(2/lambda)*2=2lambda;\nits23 entry is\n(2lambda^4+2lambda^3*q58+2lambda*q58)/lambda^2-(2/lambda)q58\n=2lambda^2+2lambda*q58.\nThe other five listed entries are obtained by division by lambda^2.\n\nApply Section3 separately to these two complete four-gradient derivatives.\nIt gives\n\n    p0=p1=p2=p4=p5=p6=0,\n    LambdaL'(0)=LambdaR'(0)=0.\n\nDifferentiating(20) now leaves\n\n    -(2lambda^2+2lambda)*p3=0,\n\nso p3=0. Then a'=b'=dhat'=kappa'=0. Equations(21) give N'=mu_j'=0,\nand differentiating(22) gives all original RR coefficient derivatives0.\nThe PP and crossing derivatives are already0. Consequently\n\n    f=0, W=0, Y|Z0=0  ==>  p=0 and Y=0.                   (25)\n\nThis conclusion was obtained by textual exact algebra only, with no rank\ncomputation, machine elimination or extra mathematical job. Together with(10)\nit identifies the FULL cone's zero-capacity equality set:\n\n    (p,Y) in C58 and S=0  iff  (p,Y)=(0,0).                (26)\n\n## 6. Uniqueness at the base, rederived from the same joint identity\n\nLet Lambda be ANY full feasible array at hbar. Put p=0 and\nY=Lambda-Lambdabar. The full flow gives f=0. Because p=0, no nonlinear term\noccurs: W(0,Y)=M(hbar,Lambda). On each of the36 zero base entries,\nY_ij=Lambda_ij>=0. Thus (0,Y) belongs to C58 and has S=0. Equation(26)\ngives Y=0. We have proved\n\n    Lambda feasible at hbar ==> Lambda=Lambdabar.          (27)\n\nThis proof covers every base certificate and every PSD rank. It does not\nassume the all-base uniqueness claim of the earlier b937bb05ba1645b1 draft.\n\n## 7. Uniform bounds and convergence for arbitrary nearby certificate choices\n\nWork inside the compact cube h_i in[1,4], which contains hbar in its interior.\nConsider the ONE scalar function F(x)=k*x^2/2, with k=1/8, minimizer0 and\nx0=1. Its actual GD positions satisfy\n\n    x_i=product_(j<i)(1-h_j/8), 1/2<=1-h_j/8<=7/8.\n\nThey strictly decrease and stay positive. If0<=i<j<=7, then\n\n    x_i-x_j >= x_i-x_(i+1)=(h_i/8)x_i >=2^-9,\n\nbecause i<=6 and x_i>=2^-6. Direct expansion of all ordered residuals gives\n\n    Q_ij=-k(1-k)(x_i-x_j)^2/2 <=-7/2^25 for i!=j.           (28)\n\nThis checks every original ordered finite pair for the same actual quadratic.\nIts gradients are g_i=k*x_i. In V_h, all terms h_i(1-h_i)g_i^2 and the\nterminal negative square are nonpositive, whereas f_i-f7<=f_i<=k/2.\nSince sum_i h_i<=28, we have V_h<=28k=7/2. Applying(2) to any feasible\nLambda, using full PSD and all its nonnegative entries, gives\n\n    (7/2^25)sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij\n       <=sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij(-Q_ij)<=V_h<=7/2.\n\nHence EVERY certificate in this cube satisfies\n\n    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij<=2^24.                             (29)\n\nThe bound is intentionally coarse and purely analytic. In particular the\nentire set of coefficient arrays is bounded independently of their selection.\n\nNow take any sequence h_k->hbar and choose any feasible Lambda_k. Eventually\nthe words lie in the cube. A subsequence of the arrays converges by(29).\nThe limit is nonnegative with zero diagonal, has the exact base flow, and has\na PSD base remainder by continuity: each quadratic form is a limit of\nnonnegative quadratic forms. Equation(27) identifies the limit as Lambdabar.\nIf uniform convergence over all selections failed, one could choose such a\nsequence with ||Lambda_k-Lambdabar||_F bounded away from0; its convergent\nsubsequence would contradict(27). Therefore\n\n    h->hbar through feasible words implies Lambda->Lambdabar\n    uniformly over ALL feasible choices of Lambda.         (30)\n\nNo estimate on ||Lambda-Lambdabar||/||h-hbar|| was used. Such quotients are\nallowed to be unbounded at this stage of the argument.\n\n## 8. Exact nonlinear remainder and strict local capacity loss\n\nFor arbitrary finite displacements p=h-hbar, Y=Lambda-Lambdabar, the full\npolynomial expansion is EXACT:\n\n    M(h,Lambda)=W(p,Y)+R2(p,Y),\n    R2(p,Y)=diag(-p0^2,...,-p6^2,-S^2)+sym(L(Y)Tp).          (31)\n\nFlow is exactly f(p,Y)=0. Every original sign is retained:\nY_ij>=0 on Z0 and Lambdabar_ij+Y_ij>=0 on E58. There are no omitted terms in\n(31). In particular the mixed coefficient-step term is retained, and higher\norders arising from a nonlinear choice of h,Lambda are already covered by\nthis exact displacement formula.\n\nPut ||(p,Y)||_* = sqrt(||p||_2^2+||Y||_F^2). Cauchy--Schwarz gives\n\n    ||L(Y)||_F <=(sqrt(7)+1)||Y||_F,\n    ||Tp||_F <=sqrt(7)||p||_2,\n    ||diag(-p_i^2,-S^2)||_F <=||p||_2^2+S^2<=8||p||_2^2.\n\nThe first bound uses seven off-diagonal entries in each row. The second uses\n||Tp||_F^2=sum_(j=0)^6(7-j)p_j^2. Symmetrization does not increase Frobenius\nnorm, and the Frobenius norm of a product is bounded by the product of norms.\nConsequently\n\n    ||R2(p,Y)||_F <=8||p||_2^2+(7+sqrt(7))||p||_2||Y||_F\n                   <=13||(p,Y)||_*^2.                     (32)\n\nDefine the compact set\n\n    K58={(p,Y) in C58: ||(p,Y)||_*=1}.\n\nIt is closed because flow and W are linear and the sign and PSD cones are\nclosed; it lies on a finite-dimensional unit sphere. It is nonempty, as the\nfollowing actual feasible path confirms without a new computation. For\n0<=t<q58 let x=q58-t. The singleton word x with multipliers2 and2(1+x) has the\nfull2-by2 remainder(2-x^2)[[1,-1],[-1,1]], which is PSD. This follows by\nsubstituting L=[[2,-2],[-2(1+x),2(1+x)]] and T=[[1,0],[x,1]] into D+sym(LT).\nJoin two copies at bridge\n1+u(1+x), then two resulting children at bridge2+x. The accepted join has\npositive coefficients, fixed right scaling lambda^2 at both levels, and\nactual word\n\n    (x,1+u(1+x),x,2+x,x,1+u(1+x),x).\n\nIts base array is Lambdabar; all coefficients are differentiable in t.\nIts tangent has p=(-1,-u,-1,-1,-1,-u,-1) and S=-lambda^2, so is nonzero\nand belongs to C58. Normalize it to obtain a point of K58.\n\nEquations(7),(26) show -S>0 everywhere on K58. Hence\n\n    m58=min_((p,Y) in K58)(-sum_i p_i)>0, c=m58/2>0.        (33)\n\nThe minimum is a mathematical definition, not a solver output or a new\ncomputation. Its positivity follows from compactness: a sequence of unit\ncone points with -S->0 would have a unit limit in the equality set(26).\n\nWe now prove that all sufficiently small FEASIBLE joint displacements obey\n\n    -S >=c||(p,Y)||_*.                                    (34)\n\nIf this failed, choose nonzero feasible displacements (p_k,Y_k) with\nt_k=||(p_k,Y_k)||_*->0 and -S_k<c*t_k. Normalize\n(p_k,Y_k)=t_k*(p_hat_k,Y_hat_k). A subsequence of the unit normalized pairs\nconverges to a pair(p_hat,Y_hat) of norm1. Exact flow and all36 active signs\npass to the limit. Homogeneity of(31) gives\n\n    M(hbar+p_k,Lambdabar+Y_k)/t_k\n       =W(p_hat_k,Y_hat_k)+t_k*R2(p_hat_k,Y_hat_k)>=0.\n\nBy(32), the second term tends to0 in norm. Thus W(p_hat,Y_hat)>=0 on the\nFULL R8, so the unit limit lies in K58. The assumed failure of(34) gives\n-sum_i p_hat_i<=c=m58/2, contradicting(33). This proves the existence of\na joint radius r58>0 on which(34) holds for every feasible pair.\n\nFinally use the uniform convergence(30) and h->hbar to choose epsilon>0\nsuch that EVERY feasible array above a word with ||h-hbar||_2<epsilon\nsatisfies ||(h-hbar,Lambda-Lambdabar)||_*<r58. The cube condition can be\nensured by making epsilon smaller, since hbar is in its interior. Equation\n(34) is then exactly the asserted capacity bound(3). At the base it holds\nwith both sides0. Cauchy--Schwarz also gives\n\n    c||Lambda-Lambdabar||_F <=Abar-A(h)\n                          =-sum_i(h_i-hbar_i)\n                          <=sqrt(7)||h-hbar||_2,\n\nwhich proves(4).\n\nThis normalization uses the JOINT displacement, so it never assumes bounded\nphysical coefficient difference quotients. Singular PSD faces and zero leading\nsigns cause no gap: PSD is passed to a limit after the full exact quadratic\nerror vanishes, and equality of capacity at a unit limiting tangent is\nexcluded by the explicit kernel calculation. No Hessian coercivity, smooth\ncertificate selection, or sufficiency of a truncated second-order path is\nassumed.\n\n## 9. It is not invoked here: Sections3--8 provide the\nspecific equality calculation and elementary compactness argument directly.\nThus there is no external theorem used as a black-box premise or requiring\nexternal_refs for this draft.\n\nThe proposed theorem is local for the FULL ORIGINAL CERTIFICATE cone. It does\nnot rule out above-threshold words outside its neighborhood, characterize the\nunrestricted worst-case minimax value, or establish the original all-n/all-word\ntarget. []}\n\n\nclass Q:\n    __slots__ = ('a', 'b')\n\n    def __init__(self, a=0, b=0):\n        self.a, self.b = Fraction(a), Fraction(b)\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def of(x):\n        if isinstance(x, Q):\n            return x\n        if isinstance(x, list):\n            return Q(*x)\n        return Q(x)\n\n    def __add__(self, other):\n        other = Q.of(other)\n        return Q(self.a + other.a, self.b + other.b)\n\n    __radd__ = __add__\n\n    def __neg__(self):\n        return Q(-self.a, -self.b)\n\n    def __sub__(self, other):\n        return self + (-Q.of(other))\n\n    def __rsub__(self, other):\n        return Q.of(other) - self\n\n    def __mul__(self, other):\n        other = Q.of(other)\n        return Q(self.a * other.a + 2 * self.b * other.b,\n                 self.a * other.b + self.b * other.a)\n\n    __rmul__ = __mul__\n\n    def __truediv__(self, other):\n        other = Q.of(other)\n        den = other.a * other.a - 2 * other.b * other.b\n        if den == 0:\n            raise ZeroDivisionError('exact zero field divisor')\n        return Q((self.a * other.a - 2 * self.b * other.b) / den,\n                 (self.b * other.a - self.a * other.b) / den)\n\n    def __pow__(self, exponent):\n        result = Q(1)\n        for _ in range(exponent):\n            result = result * self\n        return result\n\n    def __bool__(self):\n        return bool(self.a or self.b)\n\n    def __eq__(self, other):\n        other = Q.of(other)\n        return self.a == other.a and self.b == other.b\n\n    def encoded(self):\n        return [str(self.a), str(self.b)]\n\n    def sign(self):\n        a, b = self.a, self.b\n        if a == 0:\n            return (b > 0) - (b < 0)\n        if b == 0:\n            return (a > 0) - (a < 0)\n        if (a > 0) == (b > 0):\n            return 1 if a > 0 else -1\n        difference = a * a - 2 * b * b\n        return ((a > 0) - (a < 0)) * ((difference > 0) - (difference < 0))\n\n\ndef encode(x):\n    if isinstance(x, Q):\n        return x.encoded()\n    if isinstance(x, list):\n        return [encode(v) for v in x]\n    if isinstance(x, dict):\n        return {k: encode(v) for k, v in x.items()}\n    return x\n\n\ndef matrix(rows, cols):\n    return [[Q() for _ in range(cols)] for _ in range(rows)]\n\n\ndef multiply(A, B):\n    C = matrix(len(A), len(B[0]))\n    for i, row in enumerate(A):\n        for k, value in enumerate(row):\n            if value:\n                for j, other in enumerate(B[k]):\n                    if other:\n                        C[i][j] = C[i][j] + value * other\n    return C\n\n\ndef laplacian(X):\n    A = [[-x for x in row] for row in X]\n    for i in range(len(X)):\n        A[i][i] = sum(X[i], Q())\n    return A\n\n\ndef check(name, condition, detail=None):\n h = [q, Q(2), q, 1 + ell, q, Q(2), q]\n    capacity = ell ** 3\n    table = {\n        (0, 1): Q(2), (1, 0): 2 * ell, (1, 3): 2 * ell,\n        (2, 1): 2 * ell * q, (2, 3): 2 * ell ** 2 + 2 * ell * q,\n        (3, 1): 2 * ell, (3, 2): 2 * ell ** 3, (3, 7): 2 * ell ** 2,\n        (4, 3): 2 * ell * q, (4, 5): 2 * ell ** 2, (4, 7): 2 * ell * q,\n        (5, 3): 4 * ell, (5, 4): 2 * ell ** 3, (5, 7): 2 * ell ** 3 + 4 * ell,\n        (6, 3): 2 * ell * q, (6, 5): 2 * ell ** 3 * q,\n        (6, 7): 2 * ell ** 4 + 2 * ell ** 3 * q + 2 * ell * q,\n        (7, 3): 2 * ell, (7, 5): 2 * ell ** 3, (7, 6): 2 * ell ** 5,\n    }\n    pairs = [(i, j) for i in range(8) for j in range(8) if i != j]\n    zeros = [ij for ij in pairs if ij not in table]\n    base = matrix(8, 8)\n    for (i, j), value in table.items():\n        base[i][j] = value\n    L0 = laplacian(base)\n    T0 = [[Q(1) if i == j else (h[j] if j < i else Q()) for j in range(8)] for i in range(8)]\n    s = sum(h, Q())\n    omega = [-2 * x for x in h] + [2 * s]\n    base_flow = [sum((L0[i][j] for i in range(8)), Q()) - omega[j] for j in range(8)]\n    LT = multiply(L0, T0)\n    D0 = [x * (2 - x) for x in h] + [-s * (s + 2)]\n    M0 = [[(LT[i][j] + LT[j][i]) / 2 + (D0[i] if i == j else Q()) for j in range(8)] for i in range(8)]\n    check('seven steps and exact base capacity', capacity == 1 + s, h)\n    check('all twenty base   for i in range(j + 1):\n            value = svec_prices[index] if i == j else svec_prices[index] / q\n            Z[i][j] = Z[j][i] = value\n            index += 1\n    stored_Z = [[Q.of(v) for v in check('all thirty-six original zero-pair prices, no duplicates', len(data['all36_sign_prices']) == 36 and set(sigma) == set(zeros))\n    signs = [{'pair': list(ij), 'price': sigma[ij], 'sign': (4)\n\nWrite R0=s(s+2). In particular the terminal normalizers below are positive.\n\nThe full matrix equation, applied to e, gives\n\n                   L a=-2D e-T^T omega.\n\nFor i<r, (T^T omega)_i=2h_i(S_i+h_i-1), while the terminal entry is2s.\nTherefore\n\n (L a)_i=-2h_i a_i (i<r),          (L a)_r=2sA,\n                 sum_j Lambda_ij a_j=c_i a_i.          (5)\n\nThis calculation uses the full matrix equation and exact flow, rather than\nassuming a subword certificate. Since a_i>0, (5) also implies o_i>0 at every\nretained index. Zero row degree was resolved before any row normalization;\nno irreducibility assumption is used.\n\n## 3. A killed chain with all source and sink masses accounted for\n\nAdd a cemetery state dagger. From a nonterminal i<r use transitions\n\n P_ij=Lambda_ij/c_i for 0<=j<=r,\n P_i,dagger=2h_i/c_i.                                  (6)\n\nBoth r and dagger are absorbing. The row sum is1 by flow. Every retained\nnonterminal killing probability is positive. With epsilon the minimum of\nthese finitely many probabilities, the probability of surviving n more\nnonterminal transitions is at most (1-epsilon)^n. Thus absorption occurs\nalmost surely with finite mean, even if the original array is reducible or\nhas missing edges. The transient submatrix P_U, indexed by U={0,...,r-1},\nsatisfies\n\n G=sum_(n>=0)P_U^n=(Id-P_U)^(-1)>=0.                    (7)\n\nThis follows directly from the geometric row-sum bound; no inverse is\nasserted for the singular full Laplacian.\n\nSet a_dagger=0. Equation(5) says that a is harmonic at all nonterminal\nstates for (6). With boundary values a_r=A,a_dagger=0, uniqueness follows\nfrom the same row-sum bound: two solutions differ by P_U^n times their\nbounded difference, which tends to zero. Conditioning on the first\ntransition gives a harmonic hitting-probability vector, so\n\n             Prob_i(hit r before dagger)=a_i/A.        (8)\n\nStart an excursion by making one INITIAL jump from r to i<r with law\n\n                    beta_i=Lambda_ri/R0,               (9)\n\nthen run (6) until its first absorption. This initial jump is part of every\nedge reward and may not be discarded. Let N_ij be its total number of\ntraversals of the original ordered edge i->j, including the initial jump.\nThe expected visits to i<r are c_i/R0. To verify this, the occupation row\nu obeys u=beta+u P_U, whose unique solution is beta G. The candidate\nc_i/R0 obeys that equation because c_i=Lambda_ri+sum_(j<r)Lambda_ji.\nTherefore\n\n                R0 E N_ij=Lambda_ij                    (10)\n\nfor every original edge, including edges into r and the initial edges out\nof r. The chance of killing at i is 2h_i/R0. It follows that\n\n Prob(return)=s/(s+2),       Prob(killed)=2/(s+2),\n Prob(killing vertex=i | killed)=h_i/s.                (11)\n\nThese formulas also follow from (8): sum_i Lambda_ri a_i=c_r A=s^2 A.\nThus every source, return, and killing weight has been paid.\n\nFor later use put b_i=A-a_i=s-S_i on the original states; b_r=0, and set\nb_dagger=A. Both a and b are harmonic for (6), and b_i>0 for i<r.\nThe two conditional laws are explicit. Conditional on return, the initial\nlaw and subsequent transitions are\n\n beta_i^+=Lambda_ri a_i/(s^2 A),\n P_ij^+=Lambda_ij a_j/(c_i a_i),    P_i,dagger^+=0.     (12)\n\nConditional on killing, they are\n\n beta_i^-=Lambda_ri b_i/(2sA),\n P_ij^-=Lambda_ij b_j/(c_i b_i),\n P_i,dagger^-=2h_i A/(c_i b_i).                        (13)\n\nThese formulas are obtained by multiplying a transition probability by\nthe ratio of its successor's and current state's absorption probabilities.\nTheir row sums and initial sums are1 by (5) and its b counterpart. Finite\nmean absorption persists because a conditional survival probability is\nat most the original survival probability divided by the positive event\nprobability. This proves the needed stopping and summability properties\nwithout assuming reversibility or irreducibility.\n\n## 4. Both interval boundaries become a signed edge reward\n\nFix I=[l,k), 0<=l<k<=r. Define\n\n Pfx=S_l, H=S_k-S_l,\n v_l=1, v_(i+1)=-t_i v_i for l<=i<k,\n p=v_k, q=|p|=product_(i in I)t_i,\n psi_i=1 for i<l, psi_i=v_i for l<=i<k, psi_i=p for i>=k.\n\nThe desired conclusion is (1+H)q>=1, equivalently\n\n                    Delta_I=(1+H)^2 p^2-1>=0.          (14)\n\nUse formal gradients psi_i and choose x_l=1. Define formal positions and\nvalues by\n\n x_i=1+Pfx-S_i, f_i=1/2+Pfx-S_i                       (i<l),\n x_i=v_i,      f_i=v_i^2/2                           (l<=i<k),\n x_i=p(1-(S_i-S_k)), f_i=p^2/2-p^2(S_i-S_k)           (i>=k).\n\nThe original GD displacements hold, including both exterior regions.\nThe only potentially nonzero Qbar residuals are\n\n Qbar_ij=2(S_l-S_i)(1-psi_j)        (i<l,j>=l),\n Qbar_ji=2(S_j-S_k)p(psi_i-p)       (j>=k,i<k).         (15)\n\nFor a left/middle pair, put d0=S_l-S_i. The first residual expands to\n1+2d0-v_j^2-2v_j(1+d0-v_j)-(1-v_j)^2=2d0(1-v_j);\nthe reverse residual is zero. For a middle/right pair with e0=S_j-S_k,\nsubstitution gives Qbar_ij=0 and Qbar_ji=2e0 p(v_i-p).\nFor a left/right pair the two orientations give 2d0(1-p) and\n2e0 p(1-p), respectively. Within each of the three regions they vanish.\nThus all crossings are retained and neither orientation is double-counted.\n\nDefine the original boundary sums\n\n C_L=sum_(i<l,j>=l)mu_ij(S_l-S_i)(1-psi_j),\n C_R=sum_(j>=k,i<k)mu_ji(S_j-S_k)p(psi_i-p).            (16)\n\nThe value of the left side of(2) is\n\n                 (Pfx+1)^2-(Pfx+H+1)^2 p^2.\n\nHere the prefix contribution to 2sum h_i f_i+sum h_i(1-h_i)psi_i^2 is\nPfx^2+2Pfx; the interval contribution telescopes to1-p^2; with suffix\nmass Rtail=s-Pfx-H the suffix contribution is p^2(2Rtail-Rtail^2).\nThe terminal terms are -s(s+2)p^2+2sRtail p^2. Adding them proves the\ndisplayed value. Consequently\n\n Delta_I=B_I-2(C_L+C_R),\n B_I=Pfx[Pfx+2-(Pfx+2H+2)p^2].                        (17)\n\nThese are formal polynomial tests, not asserted convex interpolation data.\n\nFor an ORIGINAL Lambda edge u->w assign reward\n\n R_I(u,w)=1_(u>=l,w<l)(S_l-S_w)(1-psi_u)\n          +1_(u<k,w>=k)(S_w-S_k)p(psi_u-p).            (18)\n\nThe indicators select disjoint kinds of edges. Rewards of killing edges\nare zero. In particular an initial edge r->w with w<l has reward\n(S_l-S_w)(1-p), and must be retained. The reversal Lambda=2mu^T gives\n\n        sum_(u,w)Lambda_uw R_I(u,w)=2(C_L+C_R).         (19)\n\nLet Gamma_I be the sum of(18) over the entire killed excursion, including\nits initial terminal jump. The finite mean duration and bounded rewards\njustify summing expectations. Equation(10) yields\n\n                   2(C_L+C_R)=R0 E Gamma_I.            (20)\n\nThe exact missing estimate is therefore\n\n                         R0 E Gamma_I<=B_I.           (21)\n\nEquation(20) alone is not offered as progress closing(21).\n\n## 5. What the maximum principle actually supplies\n\nFor i<r put n_i=sum_j Lambda_ij R_I(i,j), z_i=E_i(sum of rewards before\nabsorption), and J_I=sum_i Lambda_ri R_I(r,i). Let z_r=z_dagger=0. Then\n\n z=G(n_i/c_i)_(i<r),\n R0 E Gamma_I=J_I+sum_i Lambda_ri z_i.                 (22)\n\nBecause b_dagger=A but the zero-boundary vector b_U has both terminal\nboundary coordinates set to0 in the transient equation,\n\n             (Id-P_U)b_U=(2A h_i/c_i)_(i<r).           (23)\n\nDefine the concrete scalar barrier\n\n                 theta_I=max_(i<r) n_i/(2A h_i).\n\nPositivity of G and(23) prove the valid bound\n\n             z<=theta_I b_U,\n R0 E Gamma_I<=J_I+2sA theta_I.                        (24)\n\nNo sign of theta_I is presumed. The last equality uses\nsum_i Lambda_ri b_i=2sA. This is an actual maximum-principle estimate for\nthe actual chain. Section7.2 shows that it does NOT imply(21), even for a\nlegal prefix with B_I=0: its right side is strictly positive there.\n\nThe exact discarded term can be displayed without a qualitative appeal to\npositive or negative edges. Set delta_i=2A h_i theta_I-n_i>=0. Then\n\n R0 E Gamma_I=J_I+2sA theta_I\n       -sum_i Lambda_ri [G(delta_j/c_j)_j]_i.          (25)\n\nHence repairing this barrier requires the quantitative bound\n\n sum_i Lambda_ri [G(delta_j/c_j)_j]_i\n                         >=J_I+2sA theta_I-B_I.       (26)\n\nThe occupation relation Lambda_(r,U)G=(c_i)_i makes the left side equal\nto sum_i delta_i. Thus using source conservation alone cancels the barrier\nand gives back J_I+sum_i n_i<=B_I. It supplies no new signed inequality.\nThis is the precise first failed quantitative estimate, rather than an\nassumption that every crossing edge has a favorable sign.\n\n## 6. Conditioning has a real, generally positive cost\n\nLet E_+ and E_- denote expectations for(12) and(13), respectively. Equation\n(11) gives the exact decomposition\n\n         2(C_L+C_R)=s^2 E_+ Gamma_I+2s E_- Gamma_I.     (27)\n\nFor any original edge u->w, expected traversals multiplied by the indicator\nof eventual return equal the unconditioned expected traversals multiplied\nby a_w/A. This is first-step conditioning after the traversal; absolute\nsummability permits summing it over all visits. The initial edge obeys the\nsame statement. Thus the omitted killing contribution has the exact form\n\n K_I:=2s E_- Gamma_I\n       =(1/A)sum_(u,w)Lambda_uw b_w R_I(u,w),          (28)\n\nand the return contribution has a_w in place of b_w. The weighted bound\nstill required after the Doob transform is\n\n                    s^2 E_+ Gamma_I<=B_I-K_I.          (29)\n\nThere is no theorem here making K_I nonpositive or determined by the\nkilling vertex. Section7.3 proves K_I>B_I>0 in an actual interior-interval\nexample. Section8 proves K_I=8 at a tight interval in a balanced basic\narray, and gives two killed paths with the same killing vertex but\ndifferent rewards. Replacing(29) by a free conditioning argument would\nlose these terms.\n\n## 7. Exact test on the legal array with opposite crossing signs\n\n### 7.1 Full original array and its certification\n\nLet d be the positive root of d^2+5d-4=0 and put\n\n A=5+d, kappa=A^2/16, K=kappa-1,\n h=(1+d,3/2,3/2), s=4+d.\n\nThe polynomial is strictly increasing on [0,infinity), negative at0 and\npositive at1. Hence 0<d<1, dA=4, K>0, and\n\n                       K(4-d)=2(1+d).                 (30)\n\nTo check(30), multiplying it by16 gives\n(A^2-16)(4-d)-32(1+d)=-(1+d)(d^2+5d-4)=0.\nDefine the entire original array in indices0,1,2,3 by\n\n Lambda=\n [0,      0,          0,             Kd              ]\n [3K/2,   0,          kappa,         kappa/2+3K/2     ]\n [3K/2,   7kappa/2,   0,             17kappa/2+3K/2   ]\n [K,      kappa,      14kappa,       0               ]. (31)\n\nEvery entry has the required sign. Row-minus-column sums are\nK(d-4), -3kappa+3K, -3kappa+3K, 6kappa-K(d+2), which by(30) equal\n-2(1+d),-3,-3,8+2d. Thus the full flow holds.\n\nFor the full matrix check, the child(3/2,3/2) has\n\n Lambda_child=[ [0,1,1/2], [7/2,0,17/2], [1,14,0] ],\n L_child T_child=[ [-3/4,-7/4,-1/2],\n                  [7/4,-3/4,-17/2], [1/2,17/2,15] ],\n D_child=diag(3/4,3/4,-15).\n\nIts flow is(-3,-3,6), and its symmetric matrix sum is zero. Joining the\nempty left child to this capacity4 right child with bridge1+d uses\nd(1+4+d)=4. Fact04219554e41d9750, Section11, gives\n\n V_h=kappa V_child-K[(3/2)Q_10+(3/2)Q_20+Q_30\n                           +d Q_03+(3/2)Q_13+(3/2)Q_23].\n\nInsertion of the displayed child coefficients gives exactly(31). Hence\nM=0 on all four original gradient coordinates. This verifies the example\nwithin the joint draft, without adopting the old finding as a premise.\n\nThe chain's c_0=4K, P_03=d/4, P_0,dagger=(1+d)/(2K). Their sum is1 by\n(30). Equation(12) gives P_03^+=dA/4=1: after conditioning on return,\nstate0 deterministically goes to3.\n\n### 7.2 Prefix: a valid maximum-principle bound misses the required sign\n\nTake I=[0,2). Then Pfx=0, H=5/2+d, p=d/2. Only two original edges have\nnonzero rewards and nonzero coefficients:\n\n R_I(0,3)=(3d/4)(1-d/2)>0,\n R_I(1,3)=-9d^2/8<0.                                 (32)\n\nAll initial rewards vanish. Thus n_0=(3K d^2/4)(1-d/2)>0,\nn_1=-(9d^2/8)(kappa/2+3K/2)<0, n_2=0, J_I=0. The barrier is exactly\n\n theta_I=3K d^2(1-d/2)/(8A(1+d))>0,\n J_I+2sA theta_I=3sK d^2(1-d/2)/(4(1+d))>0.           (33)\n\nThe required B_I is zero. Thus the proved comparison(24) cannot establish\nthe desired prefix sign. This is failure of a fully specified expectation\nbound, not just a failed entrywise sign claim.\n\nFor comparison the exact mean, calculated from all edges, is\n\n R0 E Gamma_I=n_0+n_1\n             =-d^2[(15+6d)K+9kappa]/16<0.             (34)\n\nThe positive path3->0->3 has probability Kd/(4R0)>0 and total reward\nR_I(0,3)>0. Thus a pathwise nonpositive-reward repair is also false.\nNo interval counterexample results: (1+H)|p|=2-3d/4>1.\nFor the entire example, the singleton products are4-3d,5/4,5/4; the\ntwo length-two products are2-3d/4 and1; the full product is Ad/4=1.\nAll six interval inequalities therefore hold.\n\n### 7.3 Interior: the killed contribution exceeds the entire budget\n\nTake I=[1,2). Write P=1+d=Pfx, H=3/2, p=-1/2. Both exterior boundaries\nare present. In(18), the nonzero coefficient-carrying rewards are\n\n R_I(2,0)=R_I(3,0)=3P/2,\n R_I(0,3)=R_I(1,3)=-9/8.                              (35)\n\nThe initial edge3->0 therefore matters. Directly,\n\n C_L=15KP/8,\n C_R=-9[2Kd+kappa+3K]/32,\n B_I=3P(P+1)/4,\n 2(C_L+C_R)=B_I-9/16.                                (36)\n\nFor the last equality, use kappa=K+1 and\nK(24+42d)=12P(P+1), which follows from(30) and d^2=4-5d.\nThe hereditary defect is exactly9/16, so this is an admissible interval.\n\nAll negative-reward edges end at3, where b_3=0. The positive edges end\nat0, where b_0=s. Therefore the actual killed contribution in(28) is\n\n                         K_I=15KP s/(4A)>0.           (37)\n\nIn fact K_I>B_I. After division by3P/4 and multiplication by(4-d)A,\nthis inequality is equivalent, using(30), to\n\n 10(1+d)(4+d)-(2+d)(4-d)(5+d)\n                       =d(32+13d+d^2)>0.              (38)\n\nConsequently the return contribution is strictly below -9/16 and is\nessential to compensate a killed contribution larger than the entire\nallowed upper bound. Positivity of a probability law cannot remove this\nsigned compensation.\n\nThere is also a direct positive killed path3->0->dagger. Its probability\nis P/(2R0)>0, and its reward is3P/2. Its reward exceeds B_I/R0 because\nB_I/R0=3P(P+1)/[4(P+3)(P+5)]<3/4 while3P/2>3/2.\nThus a uniform pathwise bound by the required mean threshold is false.\n\n## 8. Exact test on the balanced basic array with two factor channels\n\nLet w=sqrt(2), lambda=1+w, kappa=lambda^2, and h=(w,2,w). Then\ns=2lambda, A=kappa. Consider the full original array\n\n Lambda=\n [0,       2,                 0,                 0]\n [2lambda, 0,                 0,                 kappa-1]\n [0,       (kappa-1)w,        0,                 2kappa+(kappa-1)w]\n [0,       kappa-1,           2kappa lambda,      0].  (39)\n\nIts entries are nonnegative. For completeness a single step w obeys\nV_(w)=-2Q_01-2lambda Q_10, by expanding the two residuals and using\n(w-1)lambda=1. Join two such children with bridge2. Their capacities are\nlambda, and d=1 solves d(2lambda+d)=lambda^2. The accepted join gives\n\n V_h=V_left+kappa V_right\n         -(kappa-1)[w Q_21+Q_31+Q_13+w Q_23].          (40)\n\nThe coefficients after inserting the single-step identities are exactly\n(39). Thus the full polynomial identity, flow(-2w,-4,-2w,4lambda), and\nzero remainder hold on all four formal coordinates. In particular zeros\nsuch as Lambda_30 are retained; no one-factor substitution is made.\n\nTo identify the two-channel example explicitly, for i<j define an auxiliary\nchannel by\nmu_ij=(U_i-U_(i-1))(D_j+t_j D_(j+1)),\nmu_ji=(U_(i-1)+t_i U_i)(D_j-D_(j+1)), with U_-1=D_4=t_3=0.\nAt t=(w-1,1,w-1), the two choices\n\n U^(1)=(1,0,0),       (D_1^(1),D_2^(1),D_3^(1))=(lambda,0,0),\n U^(2)=(0,1,kappa+1), (D_1^(2),D_2^(2),D_3^(2))=(0,lambda,lambda)\n\ngive respectively nonzero entries mu_01=lambda,mu_10=1, and\nmu_12=lambda w,mu_13=lambda,mu_31=lambda,\nmu_23=kappa lambda,mu_32=lambda+kappa+1.\nTheir sum has Lambda=2mu^T equal to(39). The chain analysis uses the full\nsum, not a separate flow condition for either channel.\n\nTake the suffix I=[2,3). Then Pfx=w+2, H=w, p=-(w-1), and Delta_I=0.\nHere B_I=4(w+2)=4lambda w: indeed\n(3w+4)(w-1)^2=w, so the bracket in(17) equals4.\nThe ONLY edge with both nonzero coefficient and nonzero reward is the\nINITIAL edge3->1, whose reward is\n\n                  R_I(3,1)=2(1+w-1)=2w.\n\nSince Lambda_31=kappa-1=2lambda,\n\n       R0 E Gamma_I=4lambda w=B_I.                    (41)\n\nThe initial jump lands at1 with a_1=lambda and b_1=w+2=lambda w. Thus\n\n          s^2 E_+ Gamma_I=4w,\n          K_I=2s E_- Gamma_I=8.                       (42)\n\nTheir sum is4w+8=4lambda w, as required. Ignoring killed excursions would\nlose8 at an EXACTLY tight interval. This is not a small perturbation or a\nlimit, and it occurs in the genuine balanced two-channel basic array.\n\nThe killing-vertex marginal h_i/s cannot determine this reward. The two\npaths3->1->0->dagger and3->2->1->0->dagger both have positive probability:\neach specified original edge is positive in(39), and killing at0 has\npositive probability. Both are killed at0. The first has total reward2w,\nand the second has total reward0. Thus the relevant cost depends on the\nhistory even after the killing vertex is fixed.\nThe three singleton interval products of this word are1,3,1; both\nlength-two products are2w-1>1, and the full product is1. Thus it is also\nnot an interval-heredity counterexample.\n\n## 9. Remaining interface and bounded-round conclusion\n\nThe killed process is well defined for every original array satisfying(1),\nafter the proved isolation of zero coordinates. It retains the original\nterminal source, every crossing entry, both exterior boundaries, all\nalternating weights, and every absorption cost. It requires no irreducible,\nreversible, positive-support, factorized, or inherited-subword assumption.\n\nThe attempted direct estimates have the following precise status:\n\n1. Normalization, harmonic hitting probabilities, finite occupation measure,\n   conditional laws, and full coefficient recovery are derived jointly.\n2. The complete interval crossing expression is the signed excursion reward\n   in(18)--(21), not an interpolable gradient process.\n3. The concrete death-potential maximum principle(24) is valid but too weak:\n   (33) is positive where the required prefix budget is zero. Its exact\n   missing compensation is(26); source conservation by itself is circular.\n4. Conditioning on return leaves the history-dependent term(28). It is\n   larger than the entire interior budget in(37)--(38), and equals8 at the\n   tight basic interval(42). This term cannot be dropped or replaced by the\n   marginal distribution of the killing vertex.\n\nAccordingly the first unproved implication for this mechanism remains\n\n s^2 E_+ Gamma_I\n <=Pfx[Pfx+2-(Pfx+2H+2)p^2]\n      -(1/A)sum_(u,w)Lambda_uw(s-S_w)R_I(u,w),          (43)\n\nfor every interval and every full original M=0 array. Equivalently one\nmust establish(26) from more than the unsigned flow/harmonic accounting.\nNo such signed comparison is proved here, and no full-array violation of\nthe interval theorem is produced. The explicit arrays satisfy heredity.\n\nThis report does not infer a capacity ceiling, a theorem for arbitrary\nM>=0, a neighborhood theorem at seven steps, or a conclusion about the\noriginal unrestricted exact-profile Goal. The earlier scalar extremal\ndraft cannot be used downstream because its full-M0 interval hypothesis\nhas not been established. All new claims remain UNVERIFIED, with no\nfact_submit and no new accepted fact. This completes the one assigned\nmechanism/round; no additional allocation is inferred.\n\n## 10. Retrieval, verification status, and glossary\n\nThe query-memory, search-math-results, direct-proving, and\nconstruct-counterexamples procedures were used. Matlas returned two\ninapplicable results and the finite harmonic-extension Proposition12 of\nYang Chu and Robert Hough, *Solution of the 15 puzzle problem*,\narXiv:1908.07106v1 (2019), https://arxiv.org/abs/1908.07106.\nThe exact source was downloaded and read around the Markov-chain\ndefinitions, Proposition12, and the return-probability proof in Section5.\nThat proposition assumes irreducibility and cites its proof elsewhere;\nthe surrounding application uses a reversible torus walk. Those hypotheses\nare not imported. The finite absorption and harmonic uniqueness arguments\nneeded here were instead proved explicitly by(6)--(8). The reference\nprovides a proof-pattern comparison, not an external mathematical premise.\n\nOperational retrieval errors were exposed rather than treated as success:\nproject-name memory calls failed with DANUS_AGENTS_ROOT unset; the worker-\npinned calls succeeded. A lecture-note PDF download failed SSL certificate\nvalidation and web retrieval timed out. The arXiv PDF downloaded, but\npdftotext was unavailable; its source was then retrieved. Treating the\nsource as tar returned invalid header; inspection identified a single\ngzip-compressed TeX file, which was decompressed successfully and read.\nNo package was installed and no mathematical program was evaluated.\n\nGlossary for local new notation (implicit suffix kc49): L is the original\nflow Laplacian; S_i is the prefix step sum; a_i=1+S_i and b_i=s-S_i are\nthe two absorption potentials; o_i,c_i are original outgoing/incoming\nLambda sums; R0=s(s+2) is terminal departure mass; dagger is the cemetery\nstate; P is the killed transition matrix, P_U its nonterminal restriction,\nId the identity matrix, G its occupation matrix, epsilon the minimum\nkilling probability; beta is the initial terminal-departure law; N_ij is\nthe number of traversals of an original edge; superscripts+/- and E_+/E_-\ndenote conditioning on return/killing. For an interval I=[l,k), Pfx,H are\nits prefix and internal step masses; v_i is its alternating gradient test,\np its signed terminal product, q=|p|, psi its constant/alternating/constant\nextension; Delta_I is its hereditary defect, B_I the crossing budget,\nC_L,C_R the original weighted crossing sums, R_I the signed edge reward,\nGamma_I the total excursion reward, n_i the unnormalized reward drift,\nz_i its expected remaining reward, J_I the initial terminal reward,\ntheta_I the scalar death-potential barrier, delta_i its nonnegative\npointwise remainder, and K_I the killed-path reward contribution.\nIn Section7 d is the specified positive algebraic root, kappa=A^2/16,\nK=kappa-1, and P=1+d only in the interior-example calculations.\nIn Section8 w=sqrt(2),lambda=1+w,kappa=lambda^2; U^(a),D^(a) are the\ntwo explicitly displayed channel factors and do not parameterize the\ngeneral coefficient array.
