Its base array has seven positive entries\n\n    01=2, 10=13=31=2lambda, 21=2lambda*q58,\n    23=2lambda^2+2lambda*q58, 32=2lambda^3,                    (11)\n\nand all other entries0. Its remainder is0 by the accepted singleton join.\nPermit derivatives on exactly these seven pairs AND the additional pair03.\nThe latter derivative is an unrestricted real number. Write r0,r1,r2 for\nthe physical derivatives, y_ij for the coefficient derivatives, and assume\nthe four original flow derivatives hold and the full4-by4 remainder\nderivative Wloc is0. We prove all r and y are0.\n\nOnly the pairs in(11) and03 are used in the following displayed entries.\nThe entry02 of L(Lambda)T is -h2*Lambda03, whereas its entry20 is0.\nTherefore\n\n    (Wloc)_02=-q58*y03/2,\n\nwhich forces y03=0. The flow at0 and the entries00 and01 now give\n\n    y01-y10=-2r0,\n    -2q58*r0-u*y01=0,\n    -y01+2lambda*r0+u*y10=0.                               (12)\n\nThe first two equations give y10=y01+2r0 and y01=-2q58*lambda*r0.\nSubstitution in the last equation yields\n\n    [-2q58*lambda*(u-1)+4q58]r0=4lambda*r0=0.\n\nThus r0=y01=y10=0. Entries11 and13 and flow at1 give, in order,\n\n    y13=-2(1+lambda)r1,\n    y31=y13-2lambda*r1,\n    y21=2(1+lambda)r1.                                     (13)\n\nFor clarity, these equations come from\n\n    (Wloc)_11=-2(1+lambda)r1-y13,\n    2(Wloc)_13=-y13+y31+2lambda*r1,\n    y13-y21-y31=-2r1.\n\nThe12 entry, obtained from -h2*Lambda13+(h1-1)*Lambda21, gives\n\n    0=-q58*y13-2lambda*r2+y21+2lambda*q58*r1\n      =4lambda^2*r1-2lambda*r2.\n\nConsequently r2=2lambda*r1.\n\nA scalar identity supplies the last physical equation without eliminating\nany formal gradient. For a word h of length3 let v=T(h)^(-1)e. Its entries\nsatisfy v0=1 and v_(i+1)=(1-h_i)v_i. Since L(Lambda)e=0,\n\n    v^T M(h,Lambda)v=v^T D(h)v=1-A(h)^2*v3^2.               (14)\n\nThe second equality follows from\nh_i(2-h_i)v_i^2=v_i^2-v_(i+1)^2 and -s(s+2)=1-A^2.\nAt the base, M=0, A=lambda^2 and v3=-u^2. Differentiating(14) therefore\ngives the necessary equality\n\n    (r0+r1+r2)/lambda^2+lambda*(r0+r2)+r1=0.               (15)\n\nThe moving-vector derivative contributes0 because the base matrix is0.\nSubstituting r0=0 and r2=2lambda*r1 into(15) gives\n\n    [(1+2lambda)/lambda^2+2lambda^2+1]r1=0.\n\nThe bracket is strictly positive, so r1=r2=0. Equations(13) give\ny13=y31=y21=0. Entry22 now reads y21-u*y23=0, so y23=0; flow at2 gives\ny21+y23-y32=0, so y32=0. Together with(12) and y03=0, this proves that\nthe full derivative is zero. No sign assumption on these eight derivatives\nwas needed. This additional03 entry is retained because it arises when the\nright child of the seven-step array is extracted.\n\n## 4. Exact central-cut algebra to first order\n\nAssume p,Y satisfy f=0, W=0, and Y=0 on Z0. Thus all coefficient derivatives\nare supported on E58. Introduce the REAL, not assumed feasible, families\n\n    h(t)=hbar+t*p, Lambda(t)=Lambdabar+t*Y.\n\nTheir flow holds exactly for every real t. Their full remainder is O(t^2),\nentry by entry, because its constant and linear coefficients are0. Here\nO(t^2) means a function with absolute value bounded by a fixed multiple of\nt^2 for sufficiently small |t|; the constants may depend on the fixed p,Y.\nAll computations in this section retain constant and first-order terms.\n\nLet P58={0,1,2,3}, Bset={4,5,6,7}, and let eP,eB be their indicator columns\nin R8. Define capacities and bridge excess from these ACTUAL physical steps:\n\n    a(t)=1+h0+h1+h2, b(t)=1+h4+h5+h6, d(t)=h3-1.\n\nWrite h_left=(h0,h1,h2) and h_right=(h4,h5,h6). At t=0, a=b=lambda^2 and\nd=lambda. The only P58-to-Bset coefficient is\nN(t)=Lambda37. The only Bset-to-P58 coefficients are mu_j(t)=Lambda_(j,3)\nfor j=4,5,6,7. This support follows from(10) and the displayed E58, rather\nthan from an ansatz about general feasible certificates.\n\nLet LambdaL be the P58-by-P58 subarray, interpreted as a three-step certificate\narray for h0,h1,h2. Its original flow holds exactly: the first three rows\nhave no crossing edge, and the terminal flow follows by summing all four\nrow-minus-column sums. Direct matrix multiplication gives\n\n    M(h,Lambda)_(P58,P58)\n      =M(h_left,LambdaL)+(a^2-d^2-d*N)diag(0,0,0,1).        (16)\n\nIndeed the only crossing contribution within this principal block is\nN*(T_3-T_7) in row3; it changes only entry33, by -d*N. The parent-versus-child\nD difference at that entry is (1-d^2)-(1-a^2)=a^2-d^2. The other entries\nagree. Thus every derivative entry of M(h_left,LambdaL) except possibly33\nis0. The scalar flow identity e^T M e=0 from Section2 also holds for this\nchild at every t, forcing the remaining derivative entry to vanish. Hence\n\n    M(h_left,LambdaL)=O(t^2), a^2-d^2-d*N=O(t^2).            (17)\n\nFor j in Bset, direct multiplication by eP yields\n\n    2(M eP)_j=d*mu_j-N*h_j, j=4,5,6,\n    2(M eP)_7=d*mu_7-N.                                  (18)\n\nTo see every contribution in(18), T eP equals a+d on Bset and a at index3,\nso (L T eP)_j=d*mu_j. On Bset, L^T eP has only the entry -N at7.\nUpper triangular multiplication by T^T therefore contributes -N*h_j\nfor j<7 and -N at7. D eP vanishes on Bset. This proves(18) without\nany positivity or remainder-rank hypothesis.\n\nSince M=O(t^2) and d stays positive, (18) gives\n\n    mu_j=(N/d)h_j+O(t^2), j=4,5,6;\n    mu_7=N/d+O(t^2); sum_j mu_j=N*b/d+O(t^2).\n\nSumming the exact original flow over P58 gives\n\n    sum_j mu_j-N=2(a+d).\n\nIt follows that\n\n    N(b-d)=2d(a+d)+O(t^2).                                (19)\n\nMultiplying(19) by d and using d*N=a^2-d^2+O(t^2) from(17), then dividing\nby the positive a+d, gives\n\n    (a-d)(b-d)=2d^2+O(t^2),\n    a*b-d(a+b)-d^2=O(t^2).                                (20)\n\nThis saturated-bridge relation has been DERIVED for the zero-remainder\nderivative; it was not imposed on the full tangent cone.\n\n## 5. Extraction of both complete children and the full equality kernel\n\nFor positive a,b near lambda^2 define the positive root\n\n    dhat=(-(a+b)+sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab))/2,\n    Cjoin=a+b+dhat, kappa=(Cjoin/b)^2.\n\nThese functions are twice continuously differentiable for a,b in a sufficiently\nsmall positive neighborhood of their base values, so all derivatives below exist.\n\nThe identity a*b=dhat*Cjoin is exact. Equation(20) and the factorization\n\n    a*b-d(a+b)-d^2=-(d-dhat)(a+b+d+dhat)\n\nshow d-dhat=O(t^2). Consequently (17)--(19) yield\n\n    N=dhat*(kappa-1)+O(t^2),\n    mu_j=(kappa-1)h_j+O(t^2), j=4,5,6,\n    mu_7=kappa-1+O(t^2).                                  (21)\n\nFor the first equality use N=(a^2-d^2)/d+O(t^2), replace d by dhat, and\nuse a/dhat=Cjoin/b. All divisions have nonzero positive base values.\n\nDefine a real child array on Bset, with local indices0,...,3, by\n\n    LambdaR_ij=Lambda_(i,j)/kappa\n       -(1-1/kappa)h_i*1_(i<7,j=7), i,j in Bset, i!=j.     (22)\n\nKeep its diagonal0. No sign assumption on LambdaR is made. Its nonterminal\nflow is -2h_j+O(t^2): the RR subarray's flow at j<7 equals -2h_j-mu_j;\nafter division and subtraction in(22) it is\n\n    (-2h_j-mu_j)/kappa-(1-1/kappa)h_j=-2h_j+O(t^2).\n\nThe terminal flow then follows by summing the four row-minus-column sums.\n\nHere is the exact full-space composition identity used to extract remainders.\nFor our two physical child words of capacities a,b and bridge1+dhat, the\nbridge index is3 and the terminal index is7. Let j range over4,5,6, the right\nchild's nonterminal indices. Accepted fact04219554e41d9750, Section11, gives\n\n    V_parent=V_left+kappa*V_right-(kappa-1)*Ejoin,\n    Ejoin=sum_j h_j Q_(j,3)+Q_(7,3)+dhat Q_(3,7)\n                                             +sum_j h_j Q_(j,7). (23)\n\nIts coefficient proof uses dhat*(a+b+dhat)=ab and kappa=((a+b+dhat)/b)^2.\nThus the joined multiplier array consists of LambdaL on P58, kappa*LambdaR\non Bset plus (kappa-1)h_i into7 from each nonterminal right index, and\nthe cross multipliers dhat(kappa-1), (kappa-1)h_j, kappa-1. Under child flow\nthe FULL remainder is the disjoint original-coordinate block sum\nM_left plus kappa*M_right. This is an algebraic coefficient identity; its\nvalidity does not require the child arrays to be nonnegative or their\nremainders to be PSD. In the current application child flow holds through\nfirst order, which is precisely the order at which we use this identity.\nMore explicitly, for an arbitrary real child array with flow defect\nxi=L(Lambda)^T e-omega, collecting the same coefficients as in(2) gives\n\n    V+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij=trace(M Gram)\n                           +sum_i xi_i(f_i-||g_i||^2/2).\n\nThe function-value coefficients gain xi_i and the gradient diagonal gains\n-xi_i/2. This exact formula shows explicitly that an O(t^2) flow defect\ncannot alter the constant or linear matrix coefficients in the join identity.\n\nReplace the bridge by1+dhat and crossings by the exact expressions in(23).\nBy(21) these changes are O(t^2); the internal arrays are unchanged. Combining\nthe original identity(2) with(23), or just collecting its gradient coefficients,\ntherefore gives\n\n    M(h,Lambda)=M(h_left,LambdaL) direct-sum\n                   kappa*M(h_right,LambdaR)+O(t^2).        (24)\n\nThe flow errors in(22) are O(t^2), so cannot alter a constant or linear\ncoefficient in this equality. Equations(17) and(24) imply the right child\nremainder is also O(t^2). Thus both children have exact base flow, zero\nbase remainder, correct derivative flow, and zero derivative remainder.\n\nThe left child's derivative support is the seven pairs in(11). The right\nchild's support is those same seven pairs plus03. Explicitly its original\ninternal allowed pairs are45,47,54,57,65,67,75,76; the subtractions in(22)\nonly affect47,57,67. Hence no other pair is introduced. At the base,\nkappa=lambda^2 and1-1/kappa=2/lambda. Substitution in(22) gives\n\n    LambdaR_01=2, LambdaR_03=0,\n    LambdaR_10=LambdaR_13=LambdaR_31=2lambda,\n    LambdaR_21=2lambda*q58,\n    LambdaR_23=2lambda^2+2lambda*q58,\n    LambdaR_32=2lambda^3.\n\nFor example its03 entry is (2lambda*q58)/lambda^2-(2/lambda)q58=0;\nits13 entry is (2lambda^3+4lambda)/lambda^2-(2/lambda)*2=2lambda;\nits23 entry is\n(2lambda^4+2lambda^3*q58+2lambda*q58)/lambda^2-(2/lambda)q58\n=2lambda^2+2lambda*q58.\nThe other five listed entries are obtained by division by lambda^2.\n\nApply Section3 separately to these two complete four-gradient derivatives.\nIt gives\n\n    p0=p1=p2=p4=p5=p6=0,\n    LambdaL'(0)=LambdaR'(0)=0.\n\nDifferentiating(20) now leaves\n\n    -(2lambda^2+2lambda)*p3=0,\n\nso p3=0. Then a'=b'=dhat'=kappa'=0. Equations(21) give N'=mu_j'=0,\nand differentiating(22) gives all original RR coefficient derivatives0.\nThe PP and crossing derivatives are already0. Consequently\n\n    f=0, W=0, Y|Z0=0  ==>  p=0 and Y=0.                   (25)\n\nThis conclusion was obtained by textual exact algebra only, with no rank\ncomputation, machine elimination or extra mathematical job. Together with(10)\nit identifies the FULL cone's zero-capacity equality set:\n\n    (p,Y) in C58 and S=0  iff  (p,Y)=(0,0).                (26)\n\n## 6. Uniqueness at the base, rederived from the same joint identity\n\nLet Lambda be ANY full feasible array at hbar. Put p=0 and\nY=Lambda-Lambdabar. The full flow gives f=0. Because p=0, no nonlinear term\noccurs: W(0,Y)=M(hbar,Lambda). On each of the36 zero base entries,\nY_ij=Lambda_ij>=0. Thus (0,Y) belongs to C58 and has S=0. Equation(26)\ngives Y=0. We have proved\n\n    Lambda feasible at hbar ==> Lambda=Lambdabar.          (27)\n\nThis proof covers every base certificate and every PSD rank. It does not\nassume the all-base uniqueness claim of the earlier b937bb05ba1645b1 draft.\n\n## 7. Uniform bounds and convergence for arbitrary nearby certificate choices\n\nWork inside the compact cube h_i in[1,4], which contains hbar in its interior.\nConsider the ONE scalar function F(x)=k*x^2/2, with k=1/8, minimizer0 and\nx0=1. Its actual GD positions satisfy\n\n    x_i=product_(j<i)(1-h_j/8), 1/2<=1-h_j/8<=7/8.\n\nThey strictly decrease and stay positive. If0<=i<j<=7, then\n\n    x_i-x_j >= x_i-x_(i+1)=(h_i/8)x_i >=2^-9,\n\nbecause i<=6 and x_i>=2^-6. Direct expansion of all ordered residuals gives\n\n    Q_ij=-k(1-k)(x_i-x_j)^2/2 <=-7/2^25 for i!=j.           (28)\n\nThis checks every original ordered finite pair for the same actual quadratic.\nIts gradients are g_i=k*x_i. In V_h, all terms h_i(1-h_i)g_i^2 and the\nterminal negative square are nonpositive, whereas f_i-f7<=f_i<=k/2.\nSince sum_i h_i<=28, we have V_h<=28k=7/2. Applying(2) to any feasible\nLambda, using full PSD and all its nonnegative entries, gives\n\n    (7/2^25)sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij\n       <=sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij(-Q_ij)<=V_h<=7/2.\n\nHence EVERY certificate in this cube satisfies\n\n    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij<=2^24.                             (29)\n\nThe bound is intentionally coarse and purely analytic. In particular the\nentire set of coefficient arrays is bounded independently of their selection.\n\nNow take any sequence h_k->hbar and choose any feasible Lambda_k. Eventually\nthe words lie in the cube. A subsequence of the arrays converges by(29).\nThe limit is nonnegative with zero diagonal, has the exact base flow, and has\na PSD base remainder by continuity: each quadratic form is a limit of\nnonnegative quadratic forms. Equation(27) identifies the limit as Lambdabar.\nIf uniform convergence over all selections failed, one could choose such a\nsequence with ||Lambda_k-Lambdabar||_F bounded away from0; its convergent\nsubsequence would contradict(27). Therefore\n\n    h->hbar through feasible words implies Lambda->Lambdabar\n    uniformly over ALL feasible choices of Lambda.         (30)\n\nNo estimate on ||Lambda-Lambdabar||/||h-hbar|| was used. Such quotients are\nallowed to be unbounded at this stage of the argument.\n\n## 8. Exact nonlinear remainder and strict local capacity loss\n\nFor arbitrary finite displacements p=h-hbar, Y=Lambda-Lambdabar, the full\npolynomial expansion is EXACT:\n\n    M(h,Lambda)=W(p,Y)+R2(p,Y),\n    R2(p,Y)=diag(-p0^2,...,-p6^2,-S^2)+sym(L(Y)Tp).          (31)\n\nFlow is exactly f(p,Y)=0. Every original sign is retained:\nY_ij>=0 on Z0 and Lambdabar_ij+Y_ij>=0 on E58. There are no omitted terms in\n(31). In particular the mixed coefficient-step term is retained, and higher\norders arising from a nonlinear choice of h,Lambda are already covered by\nthis exact displacement formula.\n\nPut ||(p,Y)||_* = sqrt(||p||_2^2+||Y||_F^2). Cauchy--Schwarz gives\n\n    ||L(Y)||_F <=(sqrt(7)+1)||Y||_F,\n    ||Tp||_F <=sqrt(7)||p||_2,\n    ||diag(-p_i^2,-S^2)||_F <=||p||_2^2+S^2<=8||p||_2^2.\n\nThe first bound uses seven off-diagonal entries in each row. The second uses\n||Tp||_F^2=sum_(j=0)^6(7-j)p_j^2. Symmetrization does not increase Frobenius\nnorm, and the Frobenius norm of a product is bounded by the product of norms.\nConsequently\n\n    ||R2(p,Y)||_F <=8||p||_2^2+(7+sqrt(7))||p||_2||Y||_F\n                   <=13||(p,Y)||_*^2.                     (32)\n\nDefine the compact set\n\n    K58={(p,Y) in C58: ||(p,Y)||_*=1}.\n\nIt is closed because flow and W are linear and the sign and PSD cones are\nclosed; it lies on a finite-dimensional unit sphere. It is nonempty, as the\nfollowing actual feasible path confirms without a new computation. For\n0<=t<q58 let x=q58-t. The singleton word x with multipliers2 and2(1+x) has the\nfull2-by2 remainder(2-x^2)[[1,-1],[-1,1]], which is PSD. This follows by\nsubstituting L=[[2,-2],[-2(1+x),2(1+x)]] and T=[[1,0],[x,1]] into D+sym(LT).\nJoin two copies at bridge\n1+u(1+x), then two resulting children at bridge2+x. The accepted join has\npositive coefficients, fixed right scaling lambda^2 at both levels, and\nactual word\n\n    (x,1+u(1+x),x,2+x,x,1+u(1+x),x).\n\nIts base array is Lambdabar; all coefficients are differentiable in t.\nIts tangent has p=(-1,-u,-1,-1,-1,-u,-1) and S=-lambda^2, so is nonzero\nand belongs to C58. Normalize it to obtain a point of K58.\n\nEquations(7),(26) show -S>0 everywhere on K58. Hence\n\n    m58=min_((p,Y) in K58)(-sum_i p_i)>0, c=m58/2>0.        (33)\n\nThe minimum is a mathematical definition, not a solver output or a new\ncomputation. Its positivity follows from compactness: a sequence of unit\ncone points with -S->0 would have a unit limit in the equality set(26).\n\nWe now prove that all sufficiently small FEASIBLE joint displacements obey\n\n    -S >=c||(p,Y)||_*.                                    (34)\n\nIf this failed, choose nonzero feasible displacements (p_k,Y_k) with\nt_k=||(p_k,Y_k)||_*->0 and -S_k<c*t_k. Normalize\n(p_k,Y_k)=t_k*(p_hat_k,Y_hat_k). A subsequence of the unit normalized pairs\nconverges to a pair(p_hat,Y_hat) of norm1. Exact flow and all36 active signs\npass to the limit. Homogeneity of(31) gives\n\n    M(hbar+p_k,Lambdabar+Y_k)/t_k\n       =W(p_hat_k,Y_hat_k)+t_k*R2(p_hat_k,Y_hat_k)>=0.\n\nBy(32), the second term tends to0 in norm. Thus W(p_hat,Y_hat)>=0 on the\nFULL R8, so the unit limit lies in K58. The assumed failure of(34) gives\n-sum_i p_hat_i<=c=m58/2, contradicting(33). This proves the existence of\na joint radius r58>0 on which(34) holds for every feasible pair.\n\nFinally use the uniform convergence(30) and h->hbar to choose epsilon>0\nsuch that EVERY feasible array above a word with ||h-hbar||_2<epsilon\nsatisfies ||(h-hbar,Lambda-Lambdabar)||_*<r58. The cube condition can be\nensured by making epsilon smaller, since hbar is in its interior. Equation\n(34) is then exactly the asserted capacity bound(3). At the base it holds\nwith both sides0. Cauchy--Schwarz also gives\n\n    c||Lambda-Lambdabar||_F <=Abar-A(h)\n                          =-sum_i(h_i-hbar_i)\n                          <=sqrt(7)||h-hbar||_2,\n\nwhich proves(4).\n\nThis normalization uses the JOINT displacement, so it never assumes bounded\nphysical coefficient difference quotients. Singular PSD faces and zero leading\nsigns cause no gap: PSD is passed to a limit after the full exact quadratic\nerror vanishes, and equality of capacity at a unit limiting tangent is\nexcluded by the explicit kernel calculation. No Hessian coercivity, smooth\ncertificate selection, or sufficiency of a truncated second-order path is\nassumed.\n\n## 9. time.perf_counter()\nROOT = Path('/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/tangent_neighborhood_20261001')\nRESULT = {'status': 'running', 'checks': [], 'basis_readbacks': []}\n\n\nclass Q:\n    __slots__ = ('a', 'b')\n\n    def __init__(self, a=0, b=0):\n        self.a, self.b = Fraction(a), Fraction(b)\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def of(x):\n        if isinstance(x, Q):\n            return x\n        if isinstance(x, list):\n            return Q(*x)\n        return Q(x)\n\n    def __add__(self, other):\n        other = Q.of(other)\n        return Q(self.a + other.a, self.b + other.b)\n\n    __radd__ = __add__\n\n    def __neg__(self):\n        return Q(-self.a, -self.b)\n\n    def __sub__(self, other):\n        return self + (-Q.of(other))\n\n    def __rsub__(self, other):\n        return Q.of(other) - self\n\n    def __mul__(self, other):\n        other = Q.of(other)\n        return Q(self.a * other.a + 2 * self.b * other.b,\n                 self.a * other.b + self.b * other.a)\n\n    __rmul__ = __mul__\n\n    def __truediv__(self, other):\n        other = Q.of(other)\n        den = other.a * other.a - 2 * other.b * other.b\n        if den == 0:\n            raise ZeroDivisionError('exact zero field divisor')\n        return Q((self.a * other.a - 2 * self.b * other.b) / den,\n                 (self.b * other.a - self.a * other.b) / den)\n\n    def __pow__(self, exponent):\n        result = Q(1)\n        for _ in range(exponent):\n            result = result * self\n        return result\n\n    def __bool__(self):\n        return bool(self.a or self.b)\n\n    def __eq__(self, other):\n        other = Q.of(other)\n        return self.a == other.a and self.b == other.b\n\n    def encoded(self):\n        return [str(self.a), str(self.b)]\n\n    def sign(self):\n        a, b = self.a, self.b\n        if a == 0:\n            return (b > 0) - (b < 0)\n        if b == 0:\n            return (a > 0) - (a < 0)\n        if (a > 0) == (b > 0):\n            return 1 if a > 0 else -1\n        difference = a * a - 2 * b * b\n        return ((a > 0) - (a < 0)) * ((difference > 0) - (difference < 0))\n\n\ndef encode(x):\n    if isinstance(x, Q):\n        return x.encoded()\n    if isinstance(x, list):\n        return [encode(v) for v in x]\n    if isinstance(x, dict):\n        return {k: encode(v) for k, v in x.items()}\n    return x\n\n\ndef matrix(rows, cols):\n    return [[Q() for _ in range(cols)] for _ in range(rows)]\n\n\ndef multiply(A, B):\n    C = matrix(len(A), len(B[0]))\n    for i, row in enumerate(A):\n        for k, value in enumerate(row):\n            if value:\n                for j, other in enumerate(B[k]):\n                    if other:\n                        C[i][j] = C[i][j] + value * other\n    return C\n\n\ndef laplacian(X):\n    A = [[-x for x in row] for row in X]\n    for i in range(len(X)):\n        A[i][i] = sum(X[i], Q())\n    return A\n\n\ndef check(name, condition, detail=None):\n    RESULT['checks'].append({'name': name, 'passed': bool(condition), 'detail': encode(detail)})\n    if not condition:\n        raise AssertionError(name + ': ' + json.dumps(encode(detail)))\n\n\ndef main():\n    data = json.loads((ROOT / 'frozen_exact_data.json').read_text())\n    # Literal definitions from the mathematical manuscript, not from its checker.\n    q = Q(0, 1)\n    ell = 1 + q\n    h = [q, Q(2), q, 1 + ell, q, Q(2), q]\n    capacity = ell ** 3\n    table = {\n        (0, 1): Q(2), (1, 0): 2 * ell, (1, 3): 2 * ell,\n        (2, 1): 2 * ell * q, (2, 3): 2 * ell ** 2 + 2 * ell * q,\n        (3, 1): 2 * ell, (3, 2): 2 * ell ** 3, (3, 7): 2 * ell ** 2,\n        (4, 3): 2 * ell * q, (4, 5): 2 * ell ** 2, (4, 7): 2 * ell * q,\n        (5, 3): 4 * ell, (5, 4): 2 * ell ** 3, (5, 7): 2 * ell ** 3 + 4 * ell,\n        (6, 3): 2 * ell * q, (6, 5): 2 * ell ** 3 * q,\n        (6, 7): 2 * ell ** 4 + 2 * ell ** 3 * q + 2 * ell * q,\n        (7, 3): 2 * ell, (7, 5): 2 * ell ** 3, (7, 6): 2 * ell ** 5,\n    }\n    pairs = [(i, j) for i in range(8) for j in range(8) if i != j]\n    zeros = [ij for ij in pairs if ij not in table]\n    base = matrix(8, 8)\n    for (i, j), value in table.items():\n        base[i][j] = value\n    L0 = laplacian(base)\n    T0 = [[Q(1) if i == j else (h[j] if j < i else Q()) for j in range(8)] for i in range(8)]\n    s = sum(h, Q())\n    omega = [-2 * x for x in h] + [2 * s]\n    base_flow = [sum((L0[i][j] for i in range(8)), Q()) - omega[j] for j in range(8)]\n    LT = multiply(L0, T0)\n    D0 = [x * (2 - x) for x in h] + [-s * (s + 2)]\n    M0 = [[(LT[i][j] + LT[j][i]) / 2 + (D0[i] if i == j else Q()) for j in range(8)] for i in range(8)]\n    check('seven steps and exact base capacity', capacity == 1 + s, h)\n    check('all twenty base entries positive', len(table) == 20 and all(v.sign() == 1 for v in table.values()))\n    check('base original flow', all(not x for x in base_flow), base_flow)\n    check('base full remainder is zero', all(not x for row in M0 for x in row), M0)\n\n    equalities = [Q.of(v) for v in data['equality_prices']]\n    check('sixteen equality prices', len(equalities) == 16)\n    a, b = equalities[:8], equalities[8:]\n    svec_prices = [Q.of(v) for v in data['PSD_svec']]\n    check('twenty-eight svec prices', len(svec_prices) == 28)\n    Z = matrix(7, 7)\n    index = 0\n    for j in range(7):\n        for i in range(j + 1):\n            value = svec_prices[index] if i == j else svec_prices[index] / q\n            Z[i][j] = Z[j][i] = value\n            index += 1\n    stored_Z = [[Q.of(v) for v in check('all thirty-six original zero-pair prices, no duplicates', len(data['all36_sign_prices']) == 36 and set(sigma) == set(zeros))\n    signs = [{'pair': list(ij), 'price': sigma[ij], 'sign': range(7)]\n    check('every supplied LDL product entry equals Z', all(not v for row in ldl_residual for v in row), ldl_residual)\n\n    # Independent V Z V^T, where V has columns e_i-e_7.\n    H = laplacian(Y)\n        Tp = [[p[j] if j < i else Q() for j in range(8)] for i in range(8)]\n        term1, term2 = multiply(L0, Tp), multiply(LY, T0)\n        D1 = [2 * (1 - h[i]) * p[i] for i in range(7)] + [-2 * capacity * S]\n        W = [[(term1[i][j] + term2[i][j] + term1[j][i] + term2[j][i]) / 2 + (D1[i] if i == j else Q()) for j in range(8)] for i in range(8)]\n        op = [-2 * x for x in p] + [2 * S]\n        f = [sum((LY[i][j] for i in range(8)), Q()) - op[j] for j in range(8)]\n        n = [sum(W[i], Q()) for i in range(8)]\n        B = [[W[i][j] - W[i][7] - W[7][j] + W[7][7] range(8)), Q())\n        svec_B = [B[i][j] if i == j else q * B[i][j] for j in range(7) for i in range(j + 1)]\n        vector_trace = sum((x * y for x, y in zip(svec_prices, svec_B)), Q())\n        price_sign_term = sigma.get((row, col), Q()) if kind == 'Y' else Q()\n        value = trace + price_sign_term - sum((a[i] * f[i] + b[i] * n[i] for i in range(8)), Q())\n        residual = value + S\n        label = kind + str(row) + (',' + str(col) if kind == 'Y' else '')\n        common_null_residual = sum(n, Q()) - sum((f[i] * t0e[i] for i in range(8)), Q())\n        RESULT['basis_readbacks'].append(encode({'variable': label, 'S': S, 'flow': f, 'W': W, 'We': n, 'B': B, 'PSD_price': trace, 'sign_price': price_sign_term, 'stationarity_residual': residual, Original exact-profile target OPEN.", "evidence": "# A logarithmic-spiral checkpoint geometry and its paid activation obstruction\n\nStatus: JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical candidate. No numerical experiment,\nsymbolic evaluator, solver, benchmark computation, or fact_submit was used.\nThere is ONE selected replacement mechanism: change the gradient support\ncurve from a circular arc to a logarithmic spiral. The global function is\nconstructed below before its run. All new lemmas are parts of this one\ncandidate, not separately accepted facts.\n\n## 1. Target, source audit, and the changed interface\n\nFor a finite nonnegative word h of length n, let W_n(h) be the supremum of\n2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over finite-dimensional convex\ndifferentiable L-smooth functions with an attained minimum and GD\nx_(t+1)=x_t-h_t grad f(x_t)/L. Thus W=2R for the project's R.\n\nThe original benchmark remains U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. For clarity its ALL-split\ndefinition is C_0=B_0=1 and\n\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)).\n\nNo recurrence or benchmark constant is modified in this draft.\n\nPrimary source: Yuhan Ye and Kaizhao Liu, *Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal\nfor Gradient Descent*, arXiv:2609.09152v2, September10,2026,\nhttps://arxiv.org/html/2609.09152v2. The unversioned arXiv landing page\nstill identified v2 as current when read in this round. Exact HTML and its\nextracted text are in this directory's downloads/. Read portions were\nSection2, Section3.1, and the full proofs in AppendixA.1--A.3.\n\nThis paragraph is a source audit, not a project fact. The paper's Eq(1)\nuses denominator (L/2)||x_0-x_star||^2, so its symbol R denotes project W.\nIts stated Lemma2.2 retains only 1/[4(1+s_tail)]. But its actual Eq(40)\nconstructs a function with value D^2/[4(1+s_tail)] at initial distance1;\nthe paper explicitly drops the extra factor2 in passing to the lemma.\nThus the stronger prefactor 1/[2(1+s_tail)] in PROBLEM.md Section7 is\nsupported by the construction, despite the notation discrepancy.\n\nThe source takes\n\n    c_Y=sqrt(1+2 gamma+epsilon^2),\n    R_Y=sqrt(epsilon^2+(1+gamma)^2),\n    H_Y=[c_Y(1+gamma+R_Y)/(epsilon(gamma+R_Y))]^(R_Y/gamma)-1,\n    C_Y=epsilon+(H_Y+1)/epsilon.\n\nIts local steps are half the physical steps. A sufficient source choice\nis a>=2C_Y/c_Y, with selected physical steps b>=2(1+epsilon^(-2)).\nThe non-anytime recursion additionally asks a>=max{2,2B_0+2}.\n\nThere are two concrete costs. In AppendixA.1 Eq(34), the circle's normal\nrotation telescopes with exponent R_Y/gamma; its endpoint-angle ratio\ncontains 1/epsilon. In AppendixA.2 Eq(35), vanishing before activation\nrequires a bound for P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon. The ENDPOINT support plane\nalone forces this latter quantity; it is not only a loose norm estimate.\nThe source's half-sum gluing also pays its smoothness overlap and retains\nepsilon b in each transfer denominator. The new curve changes the first\nof these geometric costs. The second cost will be tested, not discarded.\n\nShared prior awareness b70f11135ec8688e and4ff405e904d7da62 described a\ncircular one-envelope relay and its minimum-reset barrier. Their claims\nare not premises here: the needed assembly and radius arguments are\nproved again below for the new curve. Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 fixes\nthe convention for all ordered interpolation residuals; Section5 below\nchecks those residuals directly. Accepted0e7e78056dc6258c rules out an\northogonal-moment shortcut; the present gradients retain correlations.\n\n## 2. Statement of the candidate checkpoint lemma\n\nFix 0<kappa<=epsilon<=1/2. Define\n\n    Theta=arctan(1/epsilon),\n    c_sp=sqrt(1+epsilon^2) exp(kappa Theta),\n    d_sp=1-kappa^2,\n    T_sp=[exp(d_sp Theta/kappa)-1]/d_sp,\n    H_sp=c_sp[1+(1+kappa)T_sp]-1,\n    A_sp=epsilon+(H_sp+1)/epsilon.                       (1)\n\nAll these quantities are independent of the word and its length.\n\nPartition ANY prescribed finite nonnegative word, in its original order,\nas\n\n    (block_0,b_0,block_1,b_1,...,block_(k-1),b_(k-1),block_k),\n\nwhere each ordinary block may be empty, s_i is its sum, and every selected\ncheckpoint satisfies b_i>=1+epsilon^(-2). The case k=0 is allowed.\nFor each i<k, Section3 constructs local vectors from the actual ordered\nsteps in block_i, with displacements P_i,Q_i satisfying\n\n    0<=P_i<=c_sp s_i,  0<=Q_i<=H_sp,  P_i>=epsilon Q_i.\n\nSet\n\n    M_i=P_i+max{epsilon b_i,epsilon+(1+Q_i)/epsilon},\n    sigma_i=1/M_i,\n    D_0=1,  D_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(b_i-1),\n    ell_0=0,  ell_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i).            (2)\n\nFor k>=1 there is one explicitly specified finite max-affine J on\nR^(k+1), and one function f=env_1 J, such that the complete physical GD\nrun starts at e_0 and has\n\n    2f(x_n)=D_k^2/(1+2s_k),\n    min f=0,\n    dist(e_0,argmin f)^2\n      =sigma_0^2[(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2].           (3)\n\nConsequently this exact true-distance payoff is\n\n    W_n(h) >= (b_0-1)^2\n               /{[(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2](1+2s_k)}\n             times product_(i=1)^(k-1)[(b_i-1)/M_i]^2. (4)\n\nIn particular the following fully explicit mass-only bound holds:\n\n    W_n(h) >= 1/(1+2s_k)\n      times product_(i=0)^(k-1)\n        [(b_i-1)/(c_sp s_i+max{epsilon b_i,A_sp})]^2. (5)\n\nFor k=0 use the one-dimensional Huber envelope, giving exactly\n1/(1+2s_0). Multiplying the displayed functions by L gives the same\nnormalized payoffs and the prescribed steps h_t/L for every L>0.\n\nThe lemma covers every word and every checkpoint selection satisfying\nits displayed condition. Zeros and arbitrarily large ordinary steps are\nincluded. No cap is imposed on selected steps. Maximizing (4) or (5)\nover allowed selections, and including k=0, is an explicit all-word\nlower-bound channel; no comparison with B_n is assumed.\n\nThere is also a direct source-format corollary, proved in Section6A:\nwith a_sp=2A_sp/c_sp and checkpoints b_i>=2(1+epsilon^(-2)),\n\n    W_n(h)>=1/[2(1+s_k)]\n        times product_(i=0)^(k-1)\n          [b_i/(c_sp(a_sp+s_i)+epsilon b_i)]^2.       (5a)\n\nIt preserves the source's numerator, terminal factor and threshold,\nwhile replacing its certified auxiliary constant. Thus the improvement\nis not obtained by concealing the b_i-1 cost of the separate formula(5).\n\n## 3. The new support curve and a backward construction for arbitrary steps\n\nFor 0<=theta<=Theta define\n\n    e(theta)=(cos theta,-sin theta),\n    e_t(theta)=(-sin theta,-cos theta),\n    R_sp(theta)=c_sp exp(-kappa theta),\n    v(theta)=R_sp(theta)e(theta),\n    n_sp(theta)=e(theta)+kappa e_t(theta).\n\nLet K be the convex hull of 0 and this compact arc. Write\nv(theta)=(p(theta),-q(theta)). The endpoint values are\nv(0)=(c_sp,0), v(Theta)=(epsilon,-1). Differentiating gives\n\n    p'=-R_sp(kappa cos theta+sin theta)<0,\n    q'= R_sp(cos theta-kappa sin theta)>=0.\n\nThe second inequality uses kappa<=epsilon=cot Theta. Hence\n\n    epsilon<=p<=c_sp, 0<=q<=1, q/p<=1/epsilon.         (6)\n\nFor delta=phi-theta in [-Theta,Theta],\n\n    <n_sp(theta),v(phi)>/R_sp(theta)\n       =exp(-kappa delta)(cos delta+kappa sin delta).\n\nThe derivative of the right side in delta is\n-(1+kappa^2)exp(-kappa delta)sin delta. Its maximum on this interval is\ntherefore1, at delta0. Since <n_sp(theta),0>=0<R_sp(theta), n_sp(theta)\nis an outward supporting normal to K at v(theta). At v(0), (0,1) is\nalso a supporting normal because every point of K has second coordinate\nnonpositive. This proves all support inequalities directly; no theorem\nabout spiral convexity is imported.\n\nNow let alpha_1,...,alpha_m be any finite nonnegative ordinary block.\nSet r_m=v_m=v(Theta) and work backward. Given r_j=(u_j,-y_j) and\nalpha=alpha_j, make the following choice.\n\nIf\n\n    y_j<=kappa[u_j+(alpha-1)c_sp],                     (7)\n\nput v_(j-1)=v(0), r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v(0).\nOtherwise choose the unique theta in (0,theta_j] satisfying\n\n    <r_j,e(theta)-kappa^(-1)e_t(theta)>/R_sp(theta)\n          =1-alpha,                                 (8)\n\nput v_(j-1)=v(theta), r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v(theta).\nHere theta_j is the angle of v_j. Once a capped vector v(0) occurs,\n(7) applies at every subsequent backward step, so (8) is only needed\nwhen theta_j>0. Equation(8) defines a number uniquely; it is not an\noracle, numerical feasibility assumption, or executed solver.\n\nWe prove by backward induction that these rules are well-defined, that\nv_j is the Euclidean projection of r_j on K, and that\n\n    u_j>=epsilon, y_j>=1, y_j/u_j<=1/epsilon.          (9)\n\nFor a noncapped point the stronger representation is\n\n    r_j=R_sp(theta_j)[(1+t_j)e(theta_j)\n                            +kappa t_j e_t(theta_j)],\n    t_j>=0.                                         (10)\n\nIt holds at j=m with theta_m=Theta,t_m=0. For a capped step let\nmu=u_j+(alpha-1)c_sp. Condition(7) implies mu>=y_j/kappa>0, and\n\n    r_(j-1)-v(0)=mu n_sp(0)+(0,kappa mu-y_j).\n\nThis is a nonnegative sum of the two supporting normals already checked,\nso it certifies projection. It also yields\ny_(j-1)<=kappa(u_(j-1)-c_sp), which implies(7) at every earlier step.\n\nIf(7) fails, the current point is not capped. By(10), its polar angle\nis at least theta_j and the left side of(8) equals1 at theta_j.\nAt theta0 the left side is (u_j-y_j/kappa)/c_sp<1-alpha.\nIts derivative is\n\n    (kappa+kappa^(-1))(u_j cos theta+y_j sin theta)\n       /R_sp(theta)>0.\n\nThus there is exactly one root theta in(0,theta_j]. The tangential\ncomponent <r_j,e_t(theta)> is nonnegative. Equation(8) says exactly\nthat r_j+(alpha-1)v(theta) is a nonnegative multiple of n_sp(theta).\nProjection and(10) follow from the supporting inequality. Adding alpha\ntimes a vector obeying(6) preserves u>=epsilon,y>=1; the ratio y/u is\na convex combination of its previous value and q/p, giving(9).\nThis completes the induction, including alpha=0.\n\nDefine\n\n    P=sum_(j=1)^m alpha_j p(theta_(j-1)),\n    Q=sum_(j=1)^m alpha_j q(theta_(j-1)), S=sum alpha_j.\n\nThen r_0=(epsilon+P,-1-Q). Bounds P<=c_sp S and P>=epsilon Q follow\nfrom(6). It remains to prove the word-independent bound for Q.\n\nConsider consecutive noncapped angles theta_old<=theta_new and put\nDelta=theta_new-theta_old. Taking the tangential component in the exact\nGD identity, and using(10), gives\n\n    t_old=B(Delta)+A(Delta)t_new,\n    A(Delta)=exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),\n    B(Delta)=exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta/kappa.          (11)\n\nFor 0<=Delta<=Theta<pi/2 both A,B are nonnegative. Put d=d_sp.\nDirect differentiation yields\n\n    A'-(d/kappa)A\n       =-(1+kappa^(-2))exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta<=0.\n\nSince A(0)=1, A<=exp(d Delta/kappa). Also B<=Delta/kappa, so\n1+dB<=1+d Delta/kappa<=exp(d Delta/kappa). Therefore(11) implies\n\n    1+d t_old<=exp(d Delta/kappa)(1+d t_new).\n\nTelescoping from theta_m=Theta,t_m=0 proves\n\n    t_j<=[exp(d(Theta-theta_j)/kappa)-1]/d<=T_sp.      (12)\n\nAt each noncapped point(10) now gives\n\n    y_j=R_sp(theta_j)[(1+t_j)sin theta_j\n                              +kappa t_j cos theta_j]\n       <=c_sp[1+(1+kappa)T_sp]=1+H_sp.\n\nA capped backward step leaves y unchanged, so the same bound holds at\nall capped points. Hence Q=y_0-1<=H_sp. Every inequality is uniform in\nm and in all finite values of the alpha_j; no maximum stepsize was used.\n\n## 4. Local finite planes and their exact zero/proximal regions\n\nFor one block suppress its index. Set\n\n    A_loc=epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon,\n    M=P+max{epsilon b,A_loc}, sigma=1/M,\n    z_circ=(1,0)-sigma(epsilon+P,-1-Q),\n    U=1-sigma(P+epsilon b)>=0, Y=sigma Q.\n\nLet V_fin consist of0,v(0),v_0,...,v_m and define\n\n    phi(z)=sigma max_(v in V_fin)<v,z-z_circ>.         (13)\n\nAll objects are fixed by the supplied block and b. The smaller convex\nhull conv(V_fin) contains every v_j and is contained in K; thus the\nprojection inequalities proved in Section3 still hold on it. At\n\n    x_j=z_circ+sigma r_j, z_j=x_j-sigma v_j,\n\nthe plane with slope sigma v_j is active in phi, giving\nsigma v_j in partial phi(z_j). Consequently z_j is the proximal point\nof phi at x_j, and grad env_1 phi(x_j)=sigma v_j.\n\nEvery nonzero v=(p,-q) obeys p>=0,q>=0,q<=p/epsilon. At the origin,\n\n    <v,-z_circ>\n     =-p+sigma[p(epsilon+P)+q(1+Q)]\n     <=-p+sigma p(P+A_loc)<=0.\n\nThus phi=0 on {z_1<=0,z_2>=0}. At(U,Y),\n\n    <v,(U,Y)-z_circ>\n       =sigma[(1-b)epsilon p+q]<=0                    (14)\n\nbecause b>=1+epsilon^(-2). Increasing its second argument preserves\nthis inequality, so phi(U,y)=0 for y>=Y.\n\nThe local GD path starts at(1,0). Its ordinary block ends at\n(U+sigma epsilon b,Y), with gradient sigma(epsilon,-1); checkpoint b\nthen reaches (U,Y+sigma b). Every ordinary/checkpoint-query proximal\npoint has first coordinate at least U and second coordinate in\n[0,sigma(1+Q)]. To check the first claim, the normal representations give\nu_j>=p_j: for an interior point this follows from(10), since\ncos theta-kappa sin theta>=0, and for a capped point it follows from\nthe normal representation. Hence\n\n    (z_j)_1-U=sigma[epsilon(b-1)+u_j-p_j]>=0.\n\nFor the second claim, (z_j)_2=sigma[1+Q-y_j+q_j]. The inequalities\n1<=y_j<=1+Q and0<=q_j<=1 give the two endpoints.\n\nThese statements concern the nonsmooth planes, precisely where the\nglobal maximum in the next section must be tested.\n\n## 5. One static global function and ALL directed interpolation rows\n\nUse coordinates0,...,k and quantities(2). For i<k put\n\n    Phi_i(z)=D_i^2 phi_i((z_i-ell_i)/D_i,z_(i+1)/D_i).\n\nSet delta_tail=D_k/(1+2s_k),\n\n    Phi_k(z)=delta_tail max{z_k-ell_k,0},\n    J(z)=max_{0<=i<=k} Phi_i(z),\n    f(x)=min_z[J(z)+||x-z||^2/2].                     (15)\n\nThis is a finite maximum of specified affine planes, including the zero\nplane. It is nonnegative and J(0)=0 by Section4 and ell_i>=0. The\nquadratic term makes the proximal minimizer unique and attained.\n\nFor completeness, the minimizing equation is x-z in partial J(z).\nThe two subgradient inequalities at z,z' give\n||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'> for g=x-z,g'=x'-z'. For an increment w,\nusing z as a trial proximal point gives\nf(x+w)<=f(x)+<g,w>+||w||^2/2. If z' is the proximal point at x+w,\nthe inequality J(z')>=J(z)+<g,z'-z> gives, by expansion,\nf(x+w)>=f(x)+<g,w>+||w-(z'-z)||^2/2. Consequently\n0<=f(x+w)-f(x)-<g,w><=||w||^2/2. This proves differentiability,\nthe supporting inequalities prove convexity, and the preceding\nmonotonicity estimate proves1-smoothness. It has minimum0 and is\neverywhere finite. No external interpolation theorem is required.\n\nWe verify the WHOLE actual trajectory. At the start of block i the\nclaimed coordinates are frozen x_j=ell_j+D_j U_j for j<i, current\ncoordinate x_i=ell_i+D_i, and all coordinates j>i equal0. Use the\nSection4 trajectory in coordinates i,i+1. At each of its candidate\nGLOBAL proximal points:\n\n* the current component Phi_i has the prescribed subgradient;\n* component i-1 has first input U_(i-1) and second input at least\n  ell_i/D_(i-1)>=Y_(i-1), so is0 by(14);\n* every earlier component is still in the same zero region;\n* component i+1 has first input at most0, because the second proximal\n  coordinate is at most D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i)=ell_(i+1), and second input0;\n* every later component is also in its pre-activation zero region.\n\nThe terminal hinge is included in this last check. Thus Phi_i dominates\nevery other component at the candidate proximal point, even if all\nvalues there are0. Its prescribed subgradient is a subgradient of J,\nproving the actual global gradient. After the checkpoint, coordinate\ni freezes at ell_i+D_i U_i, and coordinate i+1 equals\n\n    D_i(Y_i+sigma_i b_i)=ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1).\n\nThis proves the induction, without removing any old or future plane.\n\nDuring the terminal block, after accumulated steps u in[0,s_k], the\nmoving coordinate is ell_k+D_k-u delta_tail. Its proximal coordinate\nis at least ell_k because\nD_k-(u+1)delta_tail>=0. All earlier pieces remain0 there. The actual\ngradient is consequently delta_tail e_k at every terminal-block query\nand at x_n. Evaluation gives\n\n    f(x_n)=delta_tail D_k-(s_k+1/2)delta_tail^2\n           =D_k^2/[2(1+2s_k)].                        (16)\n\nHere is also an exact check of the original ALL-directed constraints.\nAt every sample, including the unqueried terminal, put g_i=grad f(x_i)\nand z_i=x_i-g_i. The preceding construction proves\ng_i in partial J(z_i) and f_i=J(z_i)+||g_i||^2/2. For every ordered\npair of samples, direct cancellation yields\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =J(z_i)-J(z_j)+<g_i,z_j-z_i><=0.              (17)\n\nThe same equation applies when either sample is any minimizer, with\ng_star=0,J(z_star)=0. Thus all remote, prefix, terminal, and optimum\nrows have been paid. Zero steps give identical actual jets and also\nsatisfy(17); no formal coordinate deletion is used to assert legality.\n\nThe Gram matrix is the Gram matrix of these explicit real vectors in\nR^(k+1), hence is positive semidefinite. Within a block the gradient\ninner products are positive multiples of cos(theta_i-theta_j)>0.\nNeighboring components share one coordinate and can have negative\ninner products. The construction is deterministic and retains these\ntemporal correlations; it averages no oracle branches.\n\n## 6. The closest minimizer and the exact normalization\n\nFor k>=1 define x_star with first two coordinates\n\n    (x_star)_0=1-sigma_0(epsilon+P_0),\n    (x_star)_1=sigma_0(1+Q_0)=ell_1,\n\nand all later coordinates0. The first component is evaluated at its\ncenter, the next has first input0, and all later pieces have nonpositive\nfirst input and zero second input. Thus J(x_star)=0. Also argmin f\nequals {J=0}, because both terms in(15) are nonnegative and attained.\n\nEvery first-component plane is active with value0 at x_star. Moreover\n\n    e_0-x_star\n      =[(P_0-epsilon Q_0)/c_sp] sigma_0(c_sp,0,0,...)\n       +(1+Q_0) sigma_0(epsilon,-1,0,...).\n\nBoth coefficients are nonnegative. Each displayed slope has nonpositive\ninner product with z-x_star for every z satisfying J(z)=0. Hence\n<e_0-x_star,z-x_star><=0. Expanding ||e_0-z||^2 proves that x_star is\nthe closest minimizer. The squared radius is exactly(3), and dividing\n(16) by it cancels sigma_0^2 to give(4).\n\nSince epsilon<=1,\n\n    sqrt((epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2)\n       <=P_0+epsilon+(1+Q_0)/epsilon<=M_0.\n\nAlso M_i<=c_sp s_i+max{epsilon b_i,A_sp}. These inequalities prove(5).\nFor k=0, J(z)=delta max{z,0} with delta=1/(1+2s_0) has closest\nminimizer0 to initial1 and gives the stated equality. All dimensions,\nminimum values, and distance factors are therefore explicit.\n\n## 6A. The same improved geometry in the original checkpoint formula\n\nThis is a second realization of the SAME spiral curve, to compare its\nauxiliary allowance with the source without changing other factors.\nIt uses a half-sum of local envelopes, as in the audited source. Here\nthe specified genuine minimizer is0 and the actual initial norm about\nthat minimizer is1. Formula(4) above separately supplies an exact\nclosest-minimizer result for the one-envelope realization.\n\nChoose a_sp>=2A_sp/c_sp. For each block i run Section3 on its ordinary\nsteps divided by2, and set S=s_i/2, b=b_i/2 and A=c_sp a_sp/2. In this\nparagraph only, choose\n\n    sigma=1/(c_sp S+epsilon b+A)\n\nin(13). This possibly smaller scale still satisfies both inequalities\nsigma(P+epsilon b)<=1 and sigma[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=1, since\nP<=c_sp S and A>=A_sp. All local gradient and zero-region proofs in\nSection4 remain valid. Write f_i=env_1 phi_i, with its resulting U_i,Y_i.\nIts local checkpoint transfer is sigma b, namely\n\n    G_i=b_i/[c_sp(a_sp+s_i)+epsilon b_i].\n\nFor this realization use fresh lengths D'_0=1,\nD'_(i+1)=D'_i G_i and thresholds ell'_0=0,\nell'_(i+1)=D'_i Y_i. Define\n\n    F(x)=(1/2)sum_(i<k) (D'_i)^2\n             f_i((x_i-ell'_i)/D'_i,x_(i+1)/D'_i)\n          +(1/2)H_delta(x_k-ell'_k),\n    delta=D'_k/(1+s_k),\n    H_delta(z)=0 (z<=0), z^2/2 (0<=z<=delta),\n               delta z-delta^2/2 (z>=delta).         (18a)\n\nEach local f_i is convex and1-smooth by the envelope proof in Section5.\nEach coordinate appears in at most two local terms, counting the\nterminal Huber term. Summing their quadratic upper bounds, with weight\n1/2, gives F(x+w)<=F(x)+<grad F(x),w>+||w||^2/2. This implies the\ngradient is1-Lipschitz: apply that upper inequality to the convex\nfunction F(z)-<grad F(y),z> at x and a unit negative-gradient step.\nSince its minimum is at y, the result is\nF(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>>=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.\nAdd the exchanged inequality and use Cauchy--Schwarz.\n\nThe same local zero regions show F>=0,F(0)=0. At the beginning of\nblock i the frozen coordinates are ell'_j+D'_j U_j for j<i, the current\ncoordinate is ell'_i+D'_i, and all later coordinates are0. During its\nlocal trajectory, coordinate i never falls below ell'_i, and coordinate\ni+1 increases from0 to D'_i Y_i before the checkpoint. Hence every old\ncomponent stays in its post-checkpoint zero region and every future\ncomponent stays in its pre-activation zero region. Only component i\nhas nonzero gradient, and its factor1/2 converts each physical step to\nthe chosen half step. The checkpoint gives the next stated starting\nstate. This verifies the entire word for the ONE function(18a).\n\nIn the terminal block the gradient is delta/2, and the final coordinate\nabove ell'_k is D'_k-delta s_k/2>=delta. Direct substitution into the\nlast Huber term gives F(x_n)=(D'_k)^2/[4(1+s_k)]. Initial x_0=e_0 and\nthe specified minimizer0 have squared distance exactly1. Therefore\nW_n(h)>=2F(x_n), proving(5a). For k=0 the same last Huber term proves\nthe empty product. The all-directed Q_ij<=0 follow from the smooth\nconvex inequality just proved, including the actual terminal and0.\nThe dimension is k+1 and all entries, including zeros, remain in the\nphysical run.\n\n## 7. Quantitative improvement and an exact finite-word obstruction\n\nFirst compare certified auxiliary costs at the SAME horizontal\ncoefficient. Take epsilon=kappa tending to0, retain c_sp from(1), and\nin the circular source choose\n\n    gamma_match=[c_sp^2-1-kappa^2]/2>0.\n\nThen c_Y=c_sp, so this does not exchange the gain for a larger linear\ncoefficient of S. Elementary logarithmic limits in the displayed\nformulas give\n\n    lim_(kappa->0) kappa log A_sp=pi/2,\n    lim_(kappa->0) kappa log(H_Y+1)/log(1/kappa)=2/pi. (18)\n\nFor the second limit, gamma_match/kappa->pi/2, R_Y->1, and the base\ninside H_Y is asymptotic to2/kappa. For the first, log A_sp differs\nfrom d_sp Theta/kappa by terms whose product with kappa tends0.\nThese are parameter asymptotics for explicit constants, not an o(1)\nloss asserted in the target theorem. In particular A_sp/C_Y tends0.\nThe new certified cost removes the factor log(1/kappa) in the exponent\nof the published circular allowance.\n\nFor the direct corollary(5a), a_sp=2A_sp/c_sp has the same limit\nkappa log a_sp->pi/2. Its ratio to the source's sufficient choice\n2C_Y/c_sp tends0. If the extra polynomial floors used by the source's\nnon-anytime recursion are imposed on both choices, these parameter\nasymptotics do not change. This draft does not import or reprove that\nrecursion's resulting exponent theorem.\n\nThat improvement is not enough to make this geometry lossless. The\nfollowing finite family tests the ACTUAL activation budget, including\nthe slack c_sp S-P, rather than merely bounding Q.\n\nFix the parameters and an integer M>=1. Set Delta=Theta/M,\ntheta_l=Theta-l Delta for0<=l<=M, and define\n\n    A_D=exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),\n    B_D=exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta/kappa,\n    t_0=0, t_l=B_D+A_D t_(l-1),\n    r_l=R_sp(theta_l)[(1+t_l)e(theta_l)\n                                   +kappa t_l e_t(theta_l)],\n    alpha_l=1-exp(-kappa Delta)cos Delta+B_D\n              +(1+kappa^2)B_D t_(l-1).              (19)\n\nEvery alpha_l is nonnegative. Tangential and radial components show\nexactly that r_l-r_(l-1)=alpha_l v(theta_l). The normal inequality\nfrom Section3 proves v(theta_l)=Pi_K(r_l). Thus the finite chronological\nword (alpha_M,...,alpha_1), started at r_M, follows these states back\nto r_0=(epsilon,-1). This is an exact family for every M, not an\nexecuted sweep or a replacement by continuous-time dynamics.\n\nWrite S_M=sum alpha_l, P_M=(r_M)_1-epsilon,\nQ_M=-(r_M)_2-1. Since theta_M=0,\n\n    P_M=c_sp(1+t_M)-epsilon,\n    1+Q_M=c_sp kappa t_M.                            (20)\n\nFor all sufficiently large M, A_D>1. The elementary geometric sum and\nthe recursion in(19) give\n\n    t_M=B_D(A_D^M-1)/(A_D-1),\n    S_M=M a_D+(1+kappa^2)B_D(t_M-MB_D)/(A_D-1),\n    a_D=1-exp(-kappa Delta)cos Delta+B_D.\n\nUsing derivatives at Delta0, or the defining limits of exp, gives\n\n    t_M -> T_sp,\n    S_M -> (1+kappa^2)T_sp/d_sp\n                 -kappa(1+kappa^2)Theta/d_sp.         (21)\n\nThe scaled endpoint plane at the origin in(13) imposes NECESSARILY\n\n    sigma^(-1)>=epsilon+P+(1+Q)/epsilon.              (22)\n\nIndeed substitute v(Theta)=(epsilon,-1) into phi(0)=0 to obtain it.\nFor the equal-angle family the additive excess left AFTER paying the\nlinear budget c_sp S_M is therefore\n\n    E_M=epsilon+P_M+(1+Q_M)/epsilon-c_sp S_M\n       -> E_sp\n        =c_sp[1+(kappa/epsilon-2kappa^2/d_sp)T_sp\n                       +kappa(1+kappa^2)Theta/d_sp]. (23)\n\nWhen epsilon=kappa<=1/2 this becomes\n\n    E_sp=c_sp[1+(1-3kappa^2)T_sp/d_sp\n                       +kappa(1+kappa^2)Theta/d_sp]>0,\n    lim_(kappa->0) kappa log E_sp=pi/2.               (24)\n\nMore precisely kappa A_sp/E_sp tends1 in this diagonal regime. The\nupper allowance and this finite-family necessary allowance therefore\nhave the same exponential scale, with their displayed bounds differing\nby a factor asymptotic to1/kappa. This is a statement about geometric\nparameter costs only.\n\nFor each fixed kappa, convergence means that actual finite words force\nE_M arbitrarily close to E_sp. Thus any universal additive allowance\nA with inverse scale c_sp S+A and the SAME endpoint/minimum-reset\nzero condition must satisfy A>=E_sp. If its denominator also contains\nepsilon b, take the legal fixed checkpoint b=1+kappa^(-2); necessarily\nA>=E_sp-kappa b, which has the same exponential parameter scale.\nThis is a quantitative obstruction to a polynomial additive activation\nallowance in this new family. It does not infer an obstruction from Q\nalone or forget the available horizontal slack.\n\nThe scope of(22)--(24) is the specified spiral support geometry with\nits actual endpoint plane and a common zero region. It says nothing\nagainst positive-height global gluings, changed endpoints, or other\nsupport curves. It does not upper-bound W_n for these words.\n\n## 8. Every paid loss and the exact remaining target obligation\n\nThere are no hidden dimension or PSD losses: dimension is k+1, the\nfunction is fixed before the run, and(17) is exact. Formula(4) uses the\ntrue closest minimizer. Passing from(4) to(5) pays only the explicitly\ndisplayed displacement bounds and the first-factor norm bound.\n\nThe remaining costs in(5) are:\n\n* the checkpoint restriction b_i>=1+epsilon^(-2);\n* the exact numerator b_i-1, caused by the future-plane proximal buffer;\n* the coefficient c_sp>1 of every s_i;\n* max{epsilon b_i,A_sp}, including unbounded selected steps;\n* the terminal Huber denominator1+2s_k, retained exactly.\n\nIn(5a) the buffer and numerator loss are replaced by the half-sum\nconstruction: the threshold is doubled, the numerator is b_i, the\ndenominator is c_sp(s_i+a_sp)+epsilon b_i, and the terminal factor is\n1/[2(1+s_k)]. Relative to the product with denominator s_i and the\nSAME terminal factor, its exact logarithmic loss, when all s_i>0, is\n\n    Loss_source=2sum_i log[c_sp+(c_sp a_sp+epsilon b_i)/s_i].\n\nThus it too retains at least2k log c_sp and an explicit, unbounded-in-b\nterm. There is no assertion that its remaining multiplicative loss\ntends1 uniformly over words.\n\nFor partitions with all s_i>0, compare(5) only as a DIAGNOSTIC with the\nformal product I=product(b_i/s_i)^2/(1+2s_k). The logarithm of I divided\nby the right side of(5) is exactly\n\n    Loss=2sum_i log[c_sp+max{epsilon b_i,A_sp}/s_i]\n            -2sum_i log(1-1/b_i).                   (25)\n\nIn particular Loss>=2k log c_sp. Also\n\n    Loss<=2k(c_sp-1)\n        +2sum_i max{epsilon b_i,A_sp}/s_i\n        +2sum_i 1/(b_i-1),                          (26)\n\nby log(1+x)<=x and -log(1-u)<=u/(1-u). These explicit sums would have\nto tend0 to use this sufficient near-unit comparison. They do not do\nso uniformly over all words: ordinary blocks can have sum0, and\nepsilon b_i/s_i is unbounded. Formula(5) itself remains defined and\nvalid when s_i=0; the diagnostic I is then simply not used. Neither\n(25) nor(26) asserts that I is the original benchmark.\n\nThe actual outstanding task is to prove, with a threshold independent\nof h, that an explicit legal adversary channel (this one, a stronger\none, or a combination with other accepted channels) reaches\n(1-delta)/B_n for every delta>0 and every sufficiently large n and\nevery h in[0,infinity)^n.
